- (direction) User: derive the MISSING Spec for pyforge-marshal — the chain (brief/PRD/architecture/epics) ran without one; Charter Lexicon 2 names the Spec the unit of contract and the chain its decomposition, not a substitute. DISTIL only; do not re-plan or invent scope.
- (decision) HEADLESS express distill (input is rich and pre-sorted: 161-line brief + 843-line PRD + 473-line architecture spine + 1024-line epics + 565-line adversarial review); no elicitation
- (decision) Slug pyforge-marshal, folder under the pyforge-marshal project's planning-artifacts/specs/ per the tracked-story-spec convention (CLAUDE.md 2026-07-25); Tier-2, durable
- (decision) Naming per Charter Branding: the eight are Smiths = agents = personas wielding Skills; 'kernel' is retired as bmad-spec tool-jargon and never appears in the artifact — this is the Spec
- (decision) A spec-pyforge-marshal Spec already exists under the local-recipes project — a narrow governance-surface Spec over .bmad-loop/** from the regenerable-factory backfill. It is NOT the product Spec and is neither edited nor moved; the relationship is recorded in sources so the two are never confused
- (decision) CAP-1..CAP-8 map 1:1 onto the PRD's eight feature groups (7.1 loop homes / 7.2 supervision / 7.3 gates / 7.4 landing / 7.5 fleet / 7.6 portability / 7.7 policy / 7.8 packaging), so every FR-1..FR-58 has exactly one capability home
- (capability) CAP-1 loop homes and isolation (FR-1..FR-8): provision an isolated, policy-composed, preflight-verified place for a loop to run and prove N of them isolated; success = idempotent init, per-home marker+symlinks agreeing, Tier-3 realpath identical to the main checkout, isolation check exit 0 for N>=2, preflight names every blocking finding
- (capability) CAP-2 run supervision (FR-9..FR-18): launch or resume a gated run detached with a supervisor attached that catches what the loop does not; success = idle strand detected and acted on before any token/time cap, ceilings stop with a named reason, escalation pauses the run, durable incremental journal survives a kill
- (capability) CAP-3 gates as first-class objects (FR-19..FR-27): a human or CI evaluates the gate standalone with no run in flight and gets a verdict that never false-greens; success = stable exit contract, frozen-surface scope check names the offending path and the story that froze it, doc-only stories do not fail on no-changes, gate mode carries its autonomy label
- (capability) CAP-4 landing with a durable paper trail (FR-28..FR-35): land a wave so every merged story's spec survives teardown without anyone remembering to copy a file; success = 100% promotion before teardown, zero-byte/truncated specs reported not promoted, conformant merge subjects, idempotent re-run, no AI-attribution in any emitted artifact
- (capability) CAP-5 fleet visibility (FR-36..FR-40): see every loop home at once, derived from ledgers rather than a hand-maintained file, with escalations surfaced first; success = one row per home with state/story/elapsed/budget, ledger-vs-git discrepancies reported as named findings, versioned machine-readable contract
- (capability) CAP-6 portability proven, not claimed (FR-41..FR-48): run the method on another agent and hold a dated artifact proving it; success = skills present in every configured adapter's declared tree, drift detector that can actually fail, conformance smoke in a throwaway home leaving no residue, matrix counting only pass
- (capability) CAP-7 policy composition (FR-49..FR-54): the memorized operating rules become layered configuration a machine enforces, with provenance; success = pure composition, per-key winning layer printed, per-story model tiering with no between-batch hand edit, invalid policy rejected at preflight, single harness seam enforced by an architectural test
- (capability) CAP-8 packaging and distribution (FR-55..FR-58): one install command yields the whole stack — the operative half of the wrap decision; success = conda package declaring the pinned harness as a run dependency plus a wheel from the same tree, marshal --version reporting both versions, upstream contribution register seeded with the five known upstream-shaped gaps
- (constraint) WRAP, never absorb (PRD 5 / AD-2) — the central decision, RESOLVED not deferred: bmad-loop is a declared runtime dependency, never vendored or patched in place; Marshal owns provisioning, supervision, gates-as-objects, landing, status and portability, not the dev/verify/review/commit engine. Evidence: the harness is already conda-packaged here so absorbing buys nothing on distribution, and all nine known gaps sit OUTSIDE the engine
- (constraint) Exactly one harness seam (AD-3 / FR-52): one module may invoke the harness binary, import its package, read its policy file or parse its output; an import-linter contract fails the build on any other reference. This is what makes the fork fallback a bounded swap rather than a rewrite
- (constraint) Marshal is harness, NOT skill (AD-1 / execution doctrine / NFR-1): no module makes a model call, loads a skill, or reads agent-authored prose as instruction; Marshal consumes only structured artifacts. The thing that governs the agent cannot be a thing the agent authors
- (constraint) Hexagonal with a pure decision core (AD-4): core/** performs no I/O, spawns no process, reads no clock; dependency direction is one-way and absolute (cli->core, cli->ports, ports<-adapters). Decisions are reproducible and testable without a running agent
- (constraint) Never false-green; unevaluable IS failure (AD-7, AD-8, AD-31, NFR-3, C-1): one owner of the verdict lattice and its exit-code projection, no code path from could-not-determine to clean, and no story reaches merged state without both a green verify and a passing scope check
- (constraint) The supervisor observes from outside and never trusts self-report (AD-9, AD-32, NFR-4): a separate OS process parented to neither the agent session nor the invoking shell, inputs externally observable only, and every enforcement ceiling reachable from externally-observed quantities alone — a wedged session must not be its own witness
- (constraint) The journal is the single source of run truth, written before the act (AD-5, AD-6, AD-30, NFR-8): every observable run fact is an entry; irreversible actions append intent before and outcome after; appends are single atomic writes. Self-owned because vendor retention is 48h-180d and transcript formats change between versions
- (constraint) Truth is partitioned by domain (AD-33): git is the sole authority for repository facts, the journal for process facts; no derived artifact sources one from the other, and disagreement is REPORTED, never silently reconciled
- (constraint) Allowlists narrow only — the governed agent never authors the allowlist it is judged against (AD-17, AD-27): effective surface = policy_surface INTERSECT spec_surface; freeze declarations, freeze removals and gate-mode changes never come from an agent-writable artifact. Story specs are machine-drafted
- (constraint) Promote before teardown, and durable off the disposable ref (AD-13, AD-29): a story spec counts as promoted only when its bytes are reachable from a ref that survives the loop home; teardown's refusal predicate is reachability computed at teardown time. Motivated by 13 of 31 warden specs lost outright plus 8 zero-byte husks
- (constraint) The loop home is the unit of isolation AND the write boundary (AD-11, AD-37, C-3, C-4): Marshal writes only to the loop home, the canonical Tier-3 store, explicitly-named promotion targets, and one enumerated machine-scoped path — never a shared cross-project file; main is never checked out in a second working tree
- (constraint) Policy is composed once, materialized, immutable for the run, and carries per-key provenance (AD-10, AD-16, AD-35): precedence is Marshal defaults -> project policy -> invocation flags, last wins, no fourth layer; no Marshal code edits a shared repo-level file to express project-specific configuration
- (constraint) Detached is the default execution mode (AD-22, FR-9): run and resume detach from the invoking shell and return a run id; foreground exists only behind an explicit flag documented unsafe for resumes — the foreground-timeout kill that ended a run mid-review becomes structurally impossible
- (constraint) Adapter behaviour comes from the declarative profile, never code branching (AD-19): everything adapter-specific is read from the harness profile plus Marshal's probe record; no 'if adapter ==' branch exists, and an unknown adapter is handled generically or reported unevaluable, never a crash
- (constraint) Every run has a ceiling and governance is structural, never conversational (C-6, NFR-5): there is no unbounded mode, and every hard limit is expressed as configuration or a deterministic check rather than an instruction to the agent — conversational safety instructions do not survive context compaction
- (constraint) Redaction is a port-boundary property applied once (AD-34, NFR-11): every port emitting bytes outside the process is a declared egress port routing through one redacting serializer; no call site redacts, and no credential reaches a journal, gate record, probe record, PR body or commit
- (constraint) One envelope for every command; machine-readable everything (AD-14, AD-39, NFR-12): identical envelope shape with coded findings from one registry, human rendering a pure projection so no human-only information exists, and status a pure function of verdict
- (constraint) Substrate and distribution posture (NFR-2, NFR-10, NFR-13, C-8, FR-57): offline by default with network use never silent; linux-64 and osx-arm64 for v1 with Windows WSL-only and stated as such; lean conda-forge-available direct dependencies; the harness pinned to a declared supported range enforced at runtime
- (constraint) No destructive default and no AI attribution (NFR-6, NFR-7, FR-35): Marshal never force-pushes, teardown refuses on unmerged work absent an explicit flag, mutating commands are idempotent and converge, and no co-author trailer, model line or generated-with line is ever added — a commit trailer is part of the permanent authorship record
- (constraint) Acceptance is deterministic and machine-checkable end to end (NFR-1): exit codes, journal entries, gate records and git history are the oracle — never an agent's assertion that it passed
- (decision) Non-goals lifted verbatim-in-substance from PRD 8 + 9.2 + the 6 portability fold: no engine reimplementation, not a skill, no self-judging (station verdicts stay independent), no hosted control plane, no IDE extension or chat participant, no HTTP proxy against any vendor inference endpoint (copilot-api bridge SUPERSEDED by the upstream copilot profile driving the sanctioned CLI), no sandbox/container, no PR lifecycle beyond open/update, no fleet-level budgets, no ACP adapter contract, no Windows-native, no OTel gen_ai.*, no formal L1-L5 story labelling, and no claim to be 'the orchestrator'

- (decision) All 6 CRITICALs from the AD-25..39 adversarial review RESOLVED 2026-07-25; verdict BLOCKED-ON -> CRITICALS-RESOLVED. F-1: .bmad-loop/policy.toml is a DERIVED artifact (AD-12) rendered from the canonical EffectivePolicy — AD-10's closing sentence amended to except a governed derived artifact, file gitignored with a meta-test, repo-wide defaults relocated to the canonical source, and new Story S-1.10 owns the conveyance that nothing owned. F-2: 'never unevaluable' was a legislated false green — replaced with SCOPED unevaluability. F-3: standalone gate evaluate folds the policy seed alone and exits 0, restoring UJ-2/FR-19; EffectivePolicy.seed_view() added (also closes F-8). F-4: trust model DECLARED as tamper-evident, not tamper-proof — the agent is inside the trust boundary, approver is attribution not authentication, process isolation named as the precondition for anything stronger. F-5: freezes split by direction — narrowing may be story-declared in any gate mode, widening needs policy or operator. F-6: composite (writer_id, counter) id, total order (ts, writer_id, counter) explicitly non-causal, and a third phase value 'observation'.
- (constraint) SEQUENCING (hard, F-1): untracking .bmad-loop/policy.toml must NOT precede S-1.10's renderer. A fresh loop home cloned without the file leaves bmad-loop with no policy at all, which is worse than the bleed. is the LAST step of S-1.10, never a preparatory one. Live hazard in the interim: loop-pyforge-herald holds that tracked file dirty (17+/27-, mode=none) and its copy still reads trigger='recommended', so a resume-and-push would publish herald-specific policy repo-wide AND silently revert the standing independent-review policy adopted the same day.
- (note) CORRECTION to the preceding constraint entry, which was written mangled: the phrase "git rm --cached is the LAST step of S-1.10" lost its subject because backticks inside a double-quoted shell string are command substitution, so bash executed the git command and interpolated its empty output (it also emitted a stray "fatal: No pathspec was given"). The memlog is append-only, so the entry stands as written and this corrects it. Intended text: the untracking step -- git rm --cached on .bmad-loop/policy.toml -- is the LAST step of S-1.10, never a preparatory one.

Package spine at src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal: core/{model,findings,verdict}.py, cli/, and the meta-tests that make AD-3/AD-4/AD-7/AD-39 build-breaking rather than memorized. 155/155 tests pass in the pyforge-marshal env, INCLUDING the import-linter gate. - (note) Publication needed an operator-assisted rebase, and that is a finding about the harness, not the story. The loop merged 1.1 to loop/pyforge-marshal correctly (satisfying AD-29 — durable off the disposable story ref) but could not publish: main had moved 3 commits ahead while the line ran, and the loop publishes with a fast-forward-only push. The wrapper documents rebase/retry on non-FF; it did not fire. Root cause was mine — I committed to main seven times while Marshal's line ran against it. Rebase was clean (no conflicts, only pixi.toml overlapped) and 155/155 passed after.
- (decision) Publishing convention corrected 2026-07-26 (PR #117): loop homes publish by pushing the loop branch and opening a PR, never by pushing to main. scripts/bmad-loop-worktree had printed as its publish line, and that hint is why ~205 commits bypassed the repo PR CI in one session. The cited constraint was over-read: main is never checked out twice is a real git limitation (two worktrees cannot hold one branch, so a loop home cannot merge into a local main) but it forbids CHECKING OUT main, not PUSHING to it. Two gates run only on a PR and a direct push runs neither: the staged-recipes linter (needs the maintenance label for anything outside recipes/) and the environment.yaml/pixi.toml sync check.
- (note) FACTUAL CORRECTION on the harness: bmad-loop does NOT publish and never pushes. Its run ends at unit-merged on the loop branch — verified by zero pushes in Marshal run 20260725-234618-4c9d logs and no HEAD:main anywhere in the bmad_loop package. Publishing is entirely the operator step, which is exactly why the line the wrapper prints is load-bearing. I had previously described the push-to-main as bmad-loop working as designed; that was wrong and is retracted.
- (decision) DEFERRED (operator, 2026-07-26): branch protection on main is held until version 1.0.0 ships. Until then the branch-and-PR convention is enforced by the wrapper printed guidance and by discipline, not by a gate. Recorded because the same session produced four bugs of the shape hardcoded-or-remembered where it should be derived-or-gated, and this is knowingly one of them — accepted deliberately with a named trigger rather than left implicit.
- (event) Epic 1 stories 1.2 / 1.3 / 1.10 SHIPPED 2026-07-29/30 via bmad-loop run on loop/pyforge-marshal, unattended (gates.mode = none) with the standing independent review pass still on every story. 34.9M weighted tokens. Landed: core/identity.py + core/policy.py + schemas/policy.json (the EffectivePolicy composition and its seed_view, closing F-8), adapters/harness_bmadloop.py write_policy_toml (S-1.10's renderer), cli/config.py, and three meta-tests (AD-23 inline key format, AD-26 seed field access, rendered-policy-untracked). 418 tests green.
- (decision) F-1 is CLOSED as of 2026-07-30, and it needed one more step than S-1.10 delivered. S-1.10 shipped the RENDERER; nothing shipped the CONVEYANCE. write_policy_toml existed but no caller reached it, no project supplied the values it would render, and the untracking that S-1.10 performed had therefore made the situation WORSE than the bleed it fixed: a loop home cloned from main would get no .bmad-loop/policy.toml at all and bmad-loop would start with no policy. The gap is the difference between 'the command exists' and 'a new loop home works'. Closed by three additions: (a) nine tracked planning-artifacts/marshal-policy.toml project-policy layers, one per station, which are the middle tier of AD-16's defaults -> project -> flags chain and the governed SOURCE the derived file is rendered from; (b) marshal config --write-harness-policy LOOP_HOME, gated on an ok composition exactly as --materialize is, reusing MRS-POLICY-004 for write failure rather than inventing a code outside REGISTERED_CODES; (c) convention-based lookup, so with no --project-policy flag the CLI resolves _bmad-output/projects/<slug>/planning-artifacts/marshal-policy.toml from the repo root and a new home works from its slug alone.
- (constraint) The nine layers were DERIVED FROM each home's live working policy.toml, not invented — the values already proven in production runs (gate_mode = none, max_followup_reviews = 2, per-station verify_commands; atlas carries two). Two keys were deliberately OMITTED rather than guessed: worktree_seed_paths, which is generated from the slug and would be a hand-copy of a derivation; and model_tier_map, whose real shape is {difficulty: {stage: model}} keyed on a difficulty vocabulary that does not exist yet (OQ Q-8 owns where a story's difficulty lives, and atlas's opus tiering is not expressible until FR-51's difficulty classes ship). A first draft used {dev, review} and was rejected by MRS-POLICY-002 — the schema caught the guess.
- (note) LIVE HAZARD on publication, stated because it is a consequence of merging, not a defect: .bmad-loop/policy.toml is still TRACKED on main, so this merge DELETES it from main's tree. The eight other loop homes hold that tracked copy checked out; their next pull will remove their working policy file. The nine committed layers are the mitigation — each home restores with `pixi run -e pyforge-marshal marshal-render-policy` or `marshal config --write-harness-policy .`. Herald is the one home where this surfaces loudly rather than silently: it holds the tracked file DIRTY (17+/27-), so the delete conflicts instead of applying. Marshal's own home already lost the file this way when 1.10's merge landed, and it was re-rendered from its layer (3962 bytes) — which is the first end-to-end proof that the conveyance works.
- (event) Mutation-tested rather than observed green, per the standing lesson about vacuous checks. Three mutants, three catches: restoring the parents[8] -> parents[7] off-by-one in repo_root (which lands on <repo>/src) failed 2 tests; removing the convention lookup failed 1; making the writer unreachable again by dropping the CLI's write_policy_toml call failed 1. 421 tests pass restored. The off-by-one is worth recording as the bug this class of seam invites: the path resolved, so nothing raised — it just pointed one directory shy of the repo root, where no project layer would ever be found.
- (decision) CORRECTION to this session's own conveyance work, made on Story 1.10's own review finding rather than on my judgement: `max_followup_reviews = 2` was moved OUT of the nine station layers and INTO `DEFAULT_POLICY` (core/policy.py). I had put it in all nine, which is nine hand-copies of a single repo-wide decision — precisely what the 1.10 follow-up review warned when it said the value 'has nothing to do with marshal' and that supplying it from a project layer under-scopes the fix. DEFAULT_POLICY is the only repo-wide home in the composition chain. Composition provenance now reports `max_followup_reviews: 2 (layer=default)`, and a NEW station inherits the value instead of having to remember it. Verified end-to-end through the real CLI, not a probe: all nine stations still render gate=none / fu=2 with per-station verify counts (atlas 2, the rest 1), so behaviour is byte-identical and only the placement changed. `gate_mode` deliberately STAYS per-station: the default is the gated `per-story-spec-approval`, so a newly added project is gated until its layer opts it into unattended — a safer posture than flipping the repo-wide default to none.
- (note) The value change required updating one test that pinned `max_followup_reviews == 1`. Recorded because 'a test failed so I changed the test' is the shape of a loosened check: the assertion still pins an exact value, the comment states why 2 is the seed, and the DW-AD23-3 incident (five still-recommended follow-up reviews damped into a gitignored ledger across atlas 10.5/10.6, marshal 1.1, warden 6.3/5.1) is named at both the assertion and the default.
