- (correction) The entry above originally read "nine id-less Story 1.7-1.9 entries" and named only that story range. It undercounted its own diff by more than half: the same pass promoted fourteen further entries from Stories 1.4-1.5 under a second banner, with no memlog entry at all, and **still missed two** (`DW-1-5-13`, `DW-1-5-14`) — both anonymous, both left behind by an id-set comparison, which is `DW-AUD-2026-07-31-3` exactly. The audit reproduced the blind spot it was reporting, and its own summary was the thing that hid it. The two are now promoted and the count corrected. Recorded rather than silently amended, because the failure mode is the point: a promotion pass that reports its own scope from memory instead of from a reconciled count cannot detect what it skipped.
- (note) The same audit confirmed that the known Marshal plan-pair staleness (`PRD.md` / `epics.md` not yet structurally re-synced to Part 5) is already recorded in `planning-artifacts/index.md` and was not duplicated as a new deferred entry. The Scribe dangling-commit incident was also found to be recovered and recorded in Scribe's memlog, so it is not a new Marshal item.
- (correction) Four "architectural synthesis" entries dated 2026-07-31 were removed from this memlog, and the seven `DW-AUD-2026-07-31-4..10` entries they announced were removed from the deferred-work ledger. Reason: they were net-new capability requirements filed as deferred work. A deferred-work entry records something a story chose not to finish and names the story that deferred it; these named a Dream as their `source_spec` and no story had ever deferred them. Filing scope as debt put roadmap into the sweep queue, double-counted it in the tech-debt tally, and routed it around the PRD and epics — where the remaining 91 Marshal stories are actually scheduled. Two were duplicates of entries already in the same file (`DW-AUD-…-6` restated `DW-1-4-9` and cited it as its own evidence; `DW-AUD-…-5` leaned the same way on `DW-1-4-4`).
- (decision) The six surviving capability proposals were re-lodged as Open Questions — SPEC OQ 17-21 and PRD Q-10..Q-14 — not as capabilities. Two of them contradict a standing Non-goal and therefore cannot become contract by being written down: the Guild-choreography proposal contradicts both *"claiming to be 'the orchestrator'"* and *"judging its own output — station verdicts stay independent"*, and the enterprise-seam proposal arrived bundled with IDE plugins and chat participants, which the Non-goals exclude outright (its server/CLI-injection half is separable and plausibly in charter). Recording them as requirements would have grown scope no decision authorized, and would have left the Spec asserting the opposite of its own Non-goals section. The §8 Non-Goals now carry inline pointers to the questions contesting them, so neither reads as settled.
- (decision) Two proposals were dropped rather than re-lodged, because the contract already holds them. The token/wall-clock budget enforcer is CAP-2, FR-13, FR-14 and constraint C-6 (*"every run has a ceiling; there is no unbounded mode"*); its fleet-level half is an explicit Non-goal with Q-4 already open against it. The cross-environment invocation port is real but is the mechanism half of Q-14, not an independent requirement — if Marshal never holds another station's verdict, it never needs the port. Note the live gap this exposes is enforcement, not specification: the session cap has run at `warn` and been exceeded six consecutive stories, which is an implementation defect against FR-13, and a ceiling that never stops at a number always exceeded is set wrong.
- (correction) Three factual errors from the same synthesis reached tracked Tier-0/Tier-2 files and are corrected. (1) **"15 pixi environments" — there are 19**; `architecture.md:506` had already corrected this count once, to 18 as of 2026-07-25, of which 6 are product envs. (2) **The eight stations are Herald · Marshal · Atlas · Warden · Mason · Doctor · Scribe · Steward** (`pyforge-charter.md:21,29`); the synthesis substituted Genesis for Marshal, writing Marshal out of the Guild in Marshal's own Dream — and Genesis is in the process of folding into the Charter with its CLI folding into Marshal (PR #177). (3) **The Charter's master pipeline flow was misquoted**: it exists at `pyforge-charter.md:595` and runs Doctor pre-flight → Herald → Marshal → Atlas → Warden → Mason → Doctor → Herald, not the sequence given. A fourth error, an "Atlas MCP server (11 tools)" alongside the CFE server, described a server that does not exist — there is one server, `conda_forge_server.py`, carrying 46 tools including the atlas surface.
- (note) Errors (1) and (2) were both present in the prompt that commissioned the synthesis and were carried through unverified into a Dream and a tracked ledger. The cheap check existed in each case — count `[environments]`, grep the Charter. The lesson is the one already recorded as `feedback_verify_the_detector_not_just_the_artifact`, one level up: a premise handed to an agent is not evidence, and an audit that inherits its facts cannot audit them.
- (decision) Apply-path correction 2026-07-31: an earlier pass in this audit hand-appended five Open Questions (numbered 17-21) directly to `SPEC.md`. That violates Charter Lexicon §2 — the Spec "is derived from an append-only memlog and re-rendered, never hand-patched" — and `docs/dreams/pr-lifecycle.md` restates the rule for exactly this Spec. The hand-edit is reverted; the questions AND their same-day resolutions enter here as amendment inputs (the PR #177 pattern), and the queued `bmad-spec` re-derivation is the application vehicle for everything below.
- (input) AMENDMENT — PR lifecycle (operator decision 2026-07-31, input Dream `docs/dreams/pr-lifecycle.md`): Marshal owns the PR lifecycle. Resolves this Spec's Open Question #10. At re-derivation the Non-goal "PR-lifecycle automation beyond opening and updating a batch PR" narrows accordingly: landing rules (required checks, merge strategy, label rules incl. this repo's `maintenance` label, env-sync trigger, branch-delete, resync) become a declared policy surface with provenance; `marshal land <story>` performs the last mile idempotently and refuses like teardown — no merge on a red required check, no silent force. Wrap-never-absorb carried unchanged: the engine keeps dev/verify/review/commit and deliberately leaves this gap open; Marshal fills it around the engine. Evidence at seeding: five PRs hand-driven in one session, one (#170) merging a real detector break because nothing in the landing path asked.
- (input) OQ input — five capability proposals from the 2026-07-31 architecture audit enter as Open Questions, each with an operator-ratified resolution recorded the same day (the five decisions below). At re-derivation each lands as a resolved question or a scoped contract change per its decision — none becomes a capability beyond what its decision states.
- (decision) Tier-2 write serialization — RESOLVED by decomposition; there is no mutex engine. Tracked Tier-2 files are per-worktree copies and git already serializes at the push/PR boundary; the real hazard is semantic lost-update through clean merges of REGENERATED artifacts (two homes regenerate the sprint ledger from different states; both merge textually clean; last wins). Rule adopted, generalizing the 2026-07-23 dashboard correction: merge append-only inputs; re-derive regenerated outputs on main after landing — an Epic 4 deploy-ordering rule, not a subsystem. The genuinely shared path is the canonical Tier-3 store (every home's backlink resolves to one realpath): concurrent appends there get an advisory file lock, a small FsPort-shaped mechanism. The journal's two-writer problem is F-6, already carried with its proposed remedy. `DW-1-4-4` (init TOCTOU) stays open on its own terms.
- (decision) Tool-surface (MCP) brokering — RESOLVED, yes, scoped. Ownership was already settled: agent-tool-surface is Marshal-owned per Charter §6. Mechanism fits the existing write boundary: the project's tool surface is declared in the PROJECT policy layer; `marshal init` renders a project-scoped `.mcp.json` into the loop home (the Story 1.7 adapter-seed pattern); preflight probes server resolvability and names blocking findings. The user-scoped `~/.claude.json` registry is never touched. Scheduling: the portability/adapter surface (Epic 6 adjacency), post-MVP, named scope.
- (decision) Escalation-knowledge capture — RESOLVED, pull model; it is forced by existing constraints. The journal owns process facts and escalations are already journaled (FR-15). Marshal's half is one small consequence at re-derivation: the FR-17 resume entry records a reference to the resolving decision/artifact. Scribe's half — ingesting resolved escalations and waivers from run journals — is a pyforge-scribe backlog story, not Marshal scope. No station writes across the boundary.
- (decision) Enterprise seam — RESOLVED by dissolution; no plugin-registry subsystem. Every named use case routes through a seam that already exists: internal MCP servers (Jira, Confluence) → the policy-declared tool surface above; proprietary and third-party agent CLIs (Devin, Copilot agents) → FR-52's declarative adapter profiles, which are designed for unknown adapters; design bridges (Figma) → Herald's craft; internal domain skills → FR-45 skill-tree projection. The one genuine residue — site-wide enterprise policy versus the no-fourth-layer constraint — resolves at install time: genesis-installer materializes site config INTO the Marshal-defaults layer, so runtime composition stays three layers and pure. IDE/chat-participant exclusion RETAINED (operator ruling 2026-07-31): the §8 Non-goal stands.
- (decision) Guild choreography — ADOPTED (operator ruling 2026-07-31): **Marshal sequences on verdicts it never authors.** Gating reads each station's durable, schema-validated verdict artifact, pinned to the tree revision it judged; Marshal never runs the judge and never alters a verdict. This CLARIFIES the two Non-goals rather than striking them: "not 'the orchestrator'" targets the engine claim (Marshal is the station around bmad-loop) and stands; "station verdicts stay independent" bars authorship, not consumption — `marshal gate evaluate` already consumes verify exit codes without being the judge. The Charter had already assigned the supervision (the master pipeline is "supervised from start to finish by the Marshal"; the Doctrine says execution has one owner; one-front-door row 11 records "own (invoke; Doctor judges Marshal's row)"). Verdict READS remove most of the cross-environment invocation-port requirement; the route-verb surface (`marshal check` / `run` / `land`) is the queued `spec-one-front-door` derivation's contract, not this Spec's.
- (note) An earlier framing in this audit said adopting choreography "requires striking both Non-goals on the record." Overstated, corrected above: the resolution clarifies their scope and strikes neither. The PRD's Q-14 wording is softened to match.
- (event) SPEC.md re-rendered 2026-07-31 from this memlog's amendment inputs (bmad-spec update): CAP-9 the-last-mile-lands-itself added (PR-lifecycle amendment; OQ 10 resolved, its non-goal struck); four constraints landed (sequence-on-verdicts; Tier-2 serialization by decomposition; policy amendment for install-time site materialization + policy-declared tool surface; escalation pull model); IDE-exclusion and not-the-orchestrator non-goals reaffirmed with clarified scope; OQ 17 added (installer verb mapping). Capability IDs 1-8 preserved; CAP-9 is the next unused id.
- (event) Self-validate pass 1 (coherence): CAP-9 carries intent+success; new constraints each rule something out (runtime site layer; station invocation as gating; merged regenerated surfaces; cross-boundary knowledge writes); non-goals remain explicit incl. one struck-with-record; success signal unchanged and testable; IDs stable. VERDICT: pass.
- (event) Self-validate pass 2 (preservation): all seven 2026-07-31 decision entries render into the kernel (five resolutions -> constraints/CAP-9/non-goal notes; PR-lifecycle amendment -> CAP-9 + OQ10 + non-goal; verb-collision -> OQ17). Stories 1.7-1.9 delivery entries are process record, not contract - correctly absent from the kernel. No load-bearing claim dropped. VERDICT: pass.
- (direction) Caller: derive the MISSING Spec for pyforge-genesis — the Charter (Lexicon §2) names the Spec as the unit of contract and the PRD/architecture/epics chain as its decomposition, not a substitute. DISTILLATION only: no re-planning, no invention.
- (decision) Headless + express distill (input is rich and pre-sorted: Dream + product brief + PRD 62 FR/20 NFR/10 SC + architecture 15 AD/12 patterns + epics 6/36). Slug pyforge-genesis; folder under the pyforge-genesis project's planning-artifacts/specs per the Tier-2 model.
- (decision) Trust the authored separation (structured upstream input): kernel-fitting content lifts into SPEC.md; the per-artifact V1 manifest inventory (a multi-item catalog) goes to a spec-authored companion. Chain docs land in sources: (fully absorbed), not companions: — the Spec is the contract, the chain is its decomposition.
- (capability) CAP-1 model-as-data: the operating model declared as one manifest keyed by stable artifact id, every entry in exactly one of five classes; adding an artifact is a manifest-only diff. Success: coverage check HARD-fails any unclassified artifact (SC-10/FR4); genesis explain prints class+rationale+update behavior (FR62).
- (capability) CAP-2 managed regions: model rule text carried inside repo-owned files as a marker-delimited tool-owned span. Success: only the span is replaced; hand-edit inside detected by body hash; marker deletion is a recorded permanent opt-out; nested/overlapping rejected; no conflict markers representable (FR43-48, NFR-R3).
- (capability) CAP-3 genesis adopt (brownfield): detect->plan->confirm->apply, dry-run default, idempotent, present-legacy preserving. Success: SC-02 empty plan against local-recipes; SC-03 second run empty/zero writes; SC-04 refuses hand-edited managed region; SC-05 refuses dirty worktree; legacy recorded and never written (FR14-22).
- (capability) CAP-4 genesis check (conformance): read-only, CI-shaped, non-zero exit on drift. Success: writes structurally unreachable (check = adopt's detect+plan); typed stable findings each with a remedy; --json; <5s on a local-recipes-sized repo; offline (FR23-28, NFR-M3, NFR-P1).
- (capability) CAP-5 genesis init (greenfield): a Dream-first repository tree from day zero — tiers, Dream seed, BMAD multi-project subtree, gitignore model region, adapters, detector in CI. Success: init then check green offline with zero network calls, under 5 min wall-clock; refuses a non-empty dir without --force (FR7-13, SC-06, SC-09).
- (capability) CAP-6 genesis update + migrations: take a later model version via a reviewable plan and version-ordered, applied-once migrations. Success: SC-07 a simulated breaking model change absorbed with no manual edits; SC-08 update provably cannot write docs/dreams/** or **/planning-artifacts/**; seeded-class artifacts are offered, never imposed (FR29-36).
- (capability) CAP-7 agent-adapter fan-out: every per-tool agent entry point derived from one neutral contract. Success: the four V1 adapters (CLAUDE.md, .cursor/rules/specs.mdc, .github/copilot-instructions.md, GEMINI.md) render from one source; a fifth is a manifest entry + wrapper template with no engine change; pre-existing adapter files receive the model as a managed region, never an overwrite (FR49-52, NFR-M1).
- (capability) CAP-8 installed state: one tool-owned, schema-validated, git-tracked state file recording what Genesis owns and what it has done. Success: records model_version/genesis_version/mode/agents/managed[]/skips[]/legacy[]/migrations_applied[]; an invalid file is a state-invalid finding, not a crash; written last in one atomic replace (FR37-42, P-08).
- (capability) CAP-9 air-gapped distribution: a conda package carrying its templates, installable and runnable behind a firewall. Success: an egress-counter test asserts zero network calls across init, adopt --dry-run, adopt --apply and check; the only network path is an explicit --template <url> (NFR-A1/A2, FR53-54, FR57).
- (constraint) Five artifact classes, closed set (PRD Extraction Manifest, FR2): REFERENCED (not materialized, version-range only) | COPIED-MANAGED (tool-owned, regenerated wholesale, check reports hand-edits) | COPIED-SEEDED (written once, repo-owned forever, never auto-touched) | GENERATED-DERIVED (recomputed every run, idempotent) | HYBRID-MANAGED-REGION (repo-owned file, tool-owned marker span). Classification rule: by who must be able to change it and how an installed repo takes a later model upgrade for it. Per-artifact V1 assignment in companion extraction-manifest.md.
- (constraint) The never-write set is structural, not conventional: docs/dreams/*.md (except the one init seed), **/planning-artifacts/** (except the init-seeded specs/README.md), **/implementation-artifacts/**, docs/specs/*.md (legacy tier), _bmad/bmm/** and _bmad/core/** (installer-owned). Upgrading the model can never touch the work made with it (FR6/FR35/SC-08).
- (constraint) The never-write guard is enforced at the LOWEST WRITE PRIMITIVE, not at call sites (NFR-R4/AD-11/P-01): every byte written to a target repo passes through one fs module holding an immutable path set frozen at construction; each write resolves to an absolute symlink-resolved path and matches BEFORE opening anything (the _bmad-output/planning-artifacts symlink would be missed unresolved); an AST meta-test enumerates write calls outside fs so the guard cannot be routed around.
- (constraint) AD-01 — CLI is typer+rich, confined to cli.py; no other module imports rich or typer, so --json output and library use stay presentation-free. Plan rendering is the product's main human surface, so warden's argparse minimalism is deliberately not adopted.
- (constraint) AD-02 — one Genesis-owned state file at .genesis/state.yml (git-tracked, schema-validated); Copier's answers file is opaque and tool-owned, never read by Genesis and written only via Copier; answers are re-supplied programmatically from Genesis state on every Copier call, so Genesis state is the single source of truth.
- (constraint) AD-03 — one canonical marker grammar rendered per comment syntax (html for .md; hash for .gitignore/.toml/.yml/shell); format is DECLARED per artifact in the manifest, never sniffed by content; the marker sha covers the region body only so the marker line is not self-referential; nested or overlapping regions are a hard error.
- (constraint) AD-04 — genesis check RE-IMPLEMENTS the generic subset of the repo's bmad_drift_check.py (the Finding shape, the HARD/DRIFT/INFO ladder, the coverage check, --json); it does not import, vendor, or extract it. ~85% of that 662-line script is local-recipes factory-specific. The two detectors coexist; convergence is explicitly out of V1.
- (constraint) AD-05 — the manifest is ONE YAML document, entries keyed by stable artifact id and never by path (paths are per-repo), each carrying class, path, format, regions+anchors, since/until model-version bounds, applies_to, and rationale. One file keeps coverage a single-pass check and makes the manifest reviewable as a diff — it IS the product's contract.
- (constraint) AD-06 — region insertion uses a declared ordered anchor list with append-at-EOF fallback and never mid-file guessing; never inserts inside a fenced code block; the chosen anchor is always reported in the plan so a human reviewing it can veto placement.
- (constraint) AD-07 — the plan is a machine-readable artifact at .genesis/plan.json, gitignored by default, carrying a repo_fingerprint (git HEAD + dirty flag + hashes of the artifacts it names); apply REFUSES a plan whose fingerprint no longer matches. Deliberately not committed (unlike Nx's migrations.json): a plan is cheap to regenerate and a committed stale plan is a hazard.
- (constraint) AD-08 — no eject verb in V1, but state must not preclude one: for every managed artifact state records id, path, class, body_sha, and inserted_region_span, which is sufficient for a future eject to strip markers and forget artifacts without touching content.
- (constraint) AD-09 — legacy conventions are preserved, recorded in state.legacy[], and at most advised (an INFO finding naming the successor); never migrated, never written to. Genesis ships no automated Tier-1 to Tier-2 migration: legacy content is the team's work product and falls inside the never-write set.
- (constraint) Every mutating verb is the same resolve->detect->plan->apply pipeline over a different input (A-01); check is adopt's detect+plan with the apply stage structurally unreachable, which is why 'check never writes' is cheap to guarantee rather than a discipline. Detect is pure (no I/O side effects); apply consumes only a serializable Plan and never re-derives state; hash guards are checked in detect, never in apply.
- (constraint) Managed-region replacement is pure byte-span substitution between markers, never a three-way merge and never semantic markdown parsing — so a half-merged file and a conflict marker are not representable states (L-05/FR44/P-06/NFR-R3).
- (constraint) Idempotence is DEFINED as plan-emptiness (AD-10): a verb is idempotent iff detect+plan immediately after a successful apply yields zero actions. This makes the local-recipes oracle (SC-02) and adopt-twice (SC-03) the same assertion against different repos — one mechanism, two proofs.
- (constraint) Copier is a dependency, not a framework (A-04/P-02/FR55): exactly one module imports copier, and only its public run_copy/run_update/run_recopy; pinned >=9.17,<10 by range with a version-range sync test. Copier's code-executing template features stay gated behind an explicit --unsafe flag (FR56/NFR-S1).
- (constraint) adopt and update are dry-run/two-phase by default and refuse on a dirty git worktree, because git is the designated undo mechanism (L-06/FR14/FR20/FR29/NFR-R2). State is written LAST, after all file writes succeed, in one atomic replace — the bmad-switch marker/symlink desync lesson encoded (P-08).
- (constraint) Two clocks (AD-05/L-04): CLI semver and operating-model semver move independently, both recorded in state; migrations are keyed to model version only, are pure plan-producing functions that never write directly, and are applied exactly once.
- (constraint) Genesis installs the machinery; Marshal operates it (L-10, PRD Boundaries). Marshal owns the SOURCE of scripts/bmad-switch and scripts/bmad-loop-worktree; Genesis owns their DELIVERY as COPIED-MANAGED artifacts and never forks them. Genesis's write scope is a repo's structure and conventions; Marshal's is a repo's executions.
- (constraint) Air-gap by construction (AD-15/P-09/D2): no module imports requests/httpx/urllib.request (meta-test enforced); templates ship in-package; the only network path is Copier's git fetch behind an explicit --template <url>. Accepted trade-off: a model change requires a package release; --template is the escape valve.
- (constraint) Packaging clones pyforge-warden's shape exactly (AD-14): pixi workspace member, hatchling, packages = [src/pyforge], genesis console entry point, and a LEAN environment with no-default-feature = true — mandatory because bmad-loop worktrees materialize the lean env, never the fat local-recipes one. Python >=3.12; pyforge.genesis shares the pyforge namespace with warden and atlas.
- (constraint) Touching root pixi.toml fires this repo's two always-on PR gates (maintenance label + regenerated environment.yaml) and stales docs/reference/library-llms-full.md; all three are acceptance criteria on the packaging work, not follow-ups (AD-14/D3).
- (constraint) Genesis's own artifacts obey the tier discipline it installs (D4): planning artifacts are Tier 2, story specs are durable and tracked under planning-artifacts/specs/, and nothing it produces may be git-tracked under implementation-artifacts/.
- (constraint) Every finding type is a member of one enum with a documented remedy string (P-10/NFR-M3); ad-hoc error strings are forbidden. Non-zero exit codes are distinct and documented per failure mode (FR61).
- (decision) Kill criteria placement (caller direction): K-01 (managed-region merge unreliable on real files) and K-02 (the local-recipes empty-plan oracle unreachable without special-casing the model into incoherence) land in the Success signal as its explicit falsifiers — both are decided by evidence the success signal already produces. K-03 (migrations cost more than hand-editing) has no threshold in any source, so it goes to open_questions rather than being invented.
- (note) Non-goals distilled from PRD 'Explicitly out of scope for V1' + architecture 'Deliberately deferred' + the three crew boundaries (Marshal operates, Herald owns deck content, Doctor owns machine health) + D5 (copier is consumed from the existing conda-forge feedstock; no recipe is authored, so CFE Rule-1/Rule-2 are not triggered by the core work).
- (assumption) Genesis targets git repositories only; non-git targets forfeit the update story entirely (PRD assumption 1).
- (assumption) The operating model has genuinely stabilized — the Dream's own gate. Evidence: pyforge-atlas shipped 32 stories and pyforge-warden 31 through it; the durable-story-specs convention closed the last known hole on 2026-07-25 (PRD assumption 2).
- (assumption) Copier's run_copy/run_update/run_recopy signatures are stable across 9.x, and its answers-file path is template-configurable (PRD assumptions 4-5; the second is AD-02's fallback trigger). Both are gated by Spike-0, which the architecture makes a CRITICAL GATE on the materialization epic rather than an accompanying task.
- (assumption) HTML-comment markers are unambiguous in the specific markdown files the manifest names (PRD assumption 6).
- (assumption) First two adopters are local-recipes (the oracle) and one greenfield pyforge sibling; external adoption is post-V1 (PRD assumption 7).
- (assumption) Marshal accepts ownership of bmad-switch / bmad-loop-worktree SOURCE while Genesis owns DELIVERY — this needs Marshal's own planning chain to agree and is not yet ratified there (PRD assumption 8 / AR-7).
- (question) K-03 has no quantified threshold in any source: at what migrations-per-model-minor-version rate does the model become too volatile to install (CM-03), and who calls it?
- (question) Does genesis init create a repository or only a tree — git init, remote, first commit, or none? The PRD scopes out 'repository creation on a git host' but leaves local git init unstated (brief OQ-3).
- (question) AD-06 specifies append-at-EOF as the anchor fallback. Is appending always safe for an unfamiliar CLAUDE.md, or does an unmatched anchor deserve a refusal instead (PRD OQ-6's residue — the architecture chose the fallback but did not close the safety question)?
- (note) PRD OQ-1..OQ-9 are all closed by AD-01..AD-09 and are therefore constraints here, not open questions. AD-04 additionally removes PRD assumption 3 (bmad_drift_check.py reuse) from the critical path.
- (event) Self-validate pass 1 (coherence, Spec Law 1-6+8): PASS — 9 capabilities all carry intent+success; intents are WHAT-only (HOW isolated to Constraints' named ADs and the companion); every constraint rules something out; 14 non-goals; success signal machine-checkable (empty plan + check exit code + no-hand-edit upgrade); CAP IDs fresh CAP-1..9, none reused.
- (event) Self-validate pass 2 (preservation): PASS after 7 repairs. Gaps found and closed: NFR-M2 (oracle in Genesis's own CI) was missing -> new constraint; FR22 (--skip honored) and NFR-P2 (<10s dry-run) -> CAP-3 success; FR60 (genesis version reports both clocks) -> two-clocks constraint; FR36 (--force -> run_recopy) -> Copier constraint; NFR-S3 (template writing outside declared paths is a hard error) -> guard constraint; NFR-R1 (transactional apply, no partial state) -> dry-run constraint; the Dream's other two faces (master narrative + alignment deck) were silently out of scope -> named explicitly in non-goals. PRD counter-metrics CM-01..CM-03 landed as the companion's Watch-metrics table rather than being dropped.
- (event) Wrapper-only content dropped (on the record, not silent): the brief's competitive comparison table (cookiecutter/cruft/Copier/projen/spec-kit positioning) and its Roadmap V1.x/V2 narrative beyond what non-goals carry; the PRD's five user journeys J1-J5 (their contract content is in CAP-3/5/6 and the legacy-preservation constraint); the epics' 6-epic/36-story decomposition and dependency-forced build order, which is the chain's job, not the contract's.
- (event) Spec finalized (create): SPEC.md + extraction-manifest.md derived from this memlog. sources: Dream + product brief + PRD + architecture + epics (fully absorbed, traceability only). surface: src/shared/packages/pyforge-genesis/** (package not yet created). 3 open questions carried: K-03 threshold, init-creates-repo-or-tree, anchor append-fallback safety.
- (gap) 2026-07-31 COVERAGE AUDIT — the V1 manifest was audited against a live inventory of what is actually installed, and classifies NONE of: skill-forge (`_bmad/skf/**`, 59 directories), bmad-builder (BMB), bmad-method-test-architecture-enterprise (TEA), bmad-creative-intelligence-suite (CIS), bmad-dashboard, bmad-manticore, bmad-labs-skills, bmad-utility-skills, bmad-method-wds-expansion, bmad-module-template. Eleven BMAD conda packages are installed; the manifest names two. This is not a manifest-completeness nit: CAP-1's success criterion is a coverage check that HARD-fails when a known artifact carries no class, so an unclassified surface is a capability that cannot be satisfied by construction. Expected disposition is REFERENCED for all of them (verified presence + floor, never materialized), matching bmad-method and bmad-loop — but the triage is deliberately NOT pre-decided here: five of them are barely exercised in this repo, and "keep, wrap, or remove" is an operator call recorded as open in docs/dreams/one-front-door.md.
- (gap) 2026-07-31 DETECTOR SET IS LISTED, NOT DERIVED — the manifest carries one COPIED detector, `scripts/bmad_drift_check.py`. The repo now has TEN, discovered from the filesystem by `scripts/detectors.py`, which itself fails when a detector script declares no scope or has no pixi task. A repo adopting the model today therefore inherits one tenth of the conformance surface, and the manifest cannot report that, because a hand-written list omits exactly the newest thing — the defect the registry was built to prevent, reproduced one tier up in the artifact that installs it. Remedy: the manifest row resolves through the registry rather than naming a file.
- (gap) 2026-07-31 HAND-WRITTEN VERSION FLOORS DRIFT — `bmad-loop` is pinned `>=0.8.1` in the REFERENCED table; 0.9.0 is the version the model is actually exercised against. A floor is a minimum, so this is not incorrect, and it is deliberately NOT bumped here: raising a floor without evidence that a newer feature is load-bearing would be inventing a requirement. Recorded because it is a restated number in a companion, which EXEMPLAR-STANDARD provenance rule 3 (*derive counts; do not restate them*) exists to prevent — the same class as the detector row above.
- (decision) 2026-07-31 THE DREAM OVER-PROMISED AND WAS CORRECTED, NOT THE SPEC — docs/dreams/genesis-installer.md listed `skill-forge` and the BMM/BMB/TEA modules among what Genesis "stands up", implying materialization. No capability and no manifest row ever claimed that; the promise existed only in Tier-0 prose. The Dream now states the two registers separately (REFERENCED = verified, never installed; COPIED/GENERATED/HYBRID = materialized). Recorded because the direction of the fix is the point: the Spec was right and the Dream was wrong, which is the reverse of the assumed failure mode and is exactly the Dream->Spec fidelity gap docs/dreams/fidelity-enforcement.md §1 names as "held but not measured" — INV-3 checks that a chain has the right SHAPE, never that it decomposes what the Dream actually promised.
- (decision) 2026-07-31 FOLD THE CLI INTO MARSHAL; RETIRE THE `genesis` BINARY (operator call). No separate `genesis` executable: the verbs become Marshal subcommands. Rationale, in order of weight. (1) NOTHING IS LEFT OF THE RELATION — the 2026-07-28 split completed on 2026-07-30; `pyforge-genesis` now holds only the two constitutive Dreams and an `epics.md` whose first line is "No epics, and that is the contract", while the installer's entire chain (Spec, manifest, PRD, architecture, 6 epics, 19 stories) sits under `pyforge-marshal`. Zero artifacts overlap. The product is named after a project explicitly separated from it, on the grounds that constitutive records and the machine that installs them are different nouns (Charter §5). (2) THE NAME RECORDS ITS ORIGIN, NOT ITS JOB — the same defect the Charter already corrected once, when `seeded`/`in-deck`/`in-spec` were retired for "naming a file or a place rather than a state". (3) THE OWNERSHIP ARGUMENT RESTED ON A SPENT VERB — this Spec's own case for Marshal ownership is that "Marshal\'s CLI cadence already opens with `marshal init`", but shipped `marshal init <slug>` provisions a LOOP HOME (story 1.4), and the Charter cadence separately uses `marshal init --spec` for a project blueprint. Three meanings of `init`, resolved by folding rather than by a fourth namespace. (4) It matches the Execution Doctrine (execution has one owner) and the runtime-orchestration Dream seeded the same day (docs/dreams/one-front-door.md).
- (open) 2026-07-31 VERB MAPPING IS NOT DECIDED — folding is agreed; the surface is not. `genesis check` and the shipped `marshal homes`/`config` are all conformance-shaped; `genesis init` collides head-on with the shipped `marshal init <slug>`; `genesis adopt`/`update`/`explain`/`version` have no collision. Candidate shapes: a `marshal repo <verb>` group that keeps the installer distinct inside one binary, or flat verbs with `init` renamed on one side. Deliberately left to the re-derivation rather than guessed here, because the 19 story keys under Epics 10-12 are named for the old verbs and renaming them is the expensive half.
- (decision by user) 2026-08-02 CONSOLIDATION (explicit user override, not a bmad-spec run): the genesis-installer Spec chain (brief, PRD, architecture, SPEC.md) is merged into this single pyforge-marshal chain. The genesis-installer memlog entries above (58, originally spec-genesis-installer/.memlog.md, 2026-07-25..2026-07-31) are replayed VERBATIM in original order and use their ORIGINAL local labels (CAP-1..CAP-9, AD-01..AD-15) as historical color -- they are NOT the live numbering. In the hand-merged, currently-live SPEC.md and architecture.md, genesis-installer's capabilities/decisions are renumbered to continue this project's own sequence: CAP-1..CAP-9 (satellite) -> CAP-10..CAP-18 (merged doc); AD-01..AD-15 (satellite architecture) -> AD-51..AD-65 (merged doc, continuing after this project's own AD-50). A future bmad-spec/bmad-architecture re-render should assign fresh sequential ids from memlog order, which naturally continues past this project's own highest existing id at replay time. epics-genesis-installer.md is OUT OF SCOPE for this consolidation and is untouched; only brief/PRD/architecture/Spec merged, per explicit user instruction.
- (decision) DRIFT-PRESUMED RECONCILED, 2026-08-09, S-13.6 (CAP-6 / FR-169). 116 entries, all under src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/** — 84 added / 32 changed / 0 removed. Every file traced to the commit that last moved it: Story 6.9 (tool-surface rendering and preflight probe) 8, Story 6.2 (skill-tree projection) 4, Story 4.4 (batch PR with hygiene preflight) 3, Story 6.5 (conformance smoke) 2, Story 5.5 (durability as a fleet-status dimension) 2, plus bmad-loop merge subjects from Epic 3's run 20260803-023308-65b7 (3-1, 3-2, 3-3, 3-4, 3-5, 3-6, 3-7) and one "fix Epic 6 closing review findings" commit. All are this station's own delivery under capabilities this Spec already contracts; every referenced story is `done` in the ledger and none is outside a contracted capability. The code caught up to a contract that was already correct — contract unchanged, baseline scoped-stamped, reasoning recorded here rather than laundered by a silent stamp.
- (note) The bmad-loop merge subjects (`Merge bmad-loop/<run>/3-4-supervisor-process-lifecycle into loop/pyforge-marshal`) DO carry their story key, but after a `/` rather than at a word boundary — a naive `(^|\s)\d+[.-]\d+` attribution regex misses all 28 of them and reports them as unattributed. Worth knowing before writing any tool that attributes files to stories by commit subject.
- (change) S-3.9 SHIPPED, 2026-08-09 (FR-170, AD-46). Reconciles by name: src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/supervisor/__main__.py (the `_classify_retired_branch` gate before the per-story push), src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/core/findings.py and src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/core/verdict.py (MRS-SUPV-009 registered in BOTH registries — REGISTERED_CODES and the verdict table; the exact-contents guard in tests refuses silent additions, which is why a third edit was needed), src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_supervisor.py (three tests + FakeVcs made conforming) and src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_findings.py (the registry's exact-contents assertion).
- (decision) MRS-SUPV-009 is a NEW code, deliberately not a reuse of MRS-SUPV-008, and the distinction IS the story. 008 was firing on the SUCCESS path: bmad-loop deletes a story's branch when the story merges into the station branch, and the supervisor polls, so the branch is routinely gone by the time the boundary is acted on. Measured 2026-08-09 on the live doctor Epic 6 run — 6 of 22 stage-pushes reported push-failed, every one on work already safe on origin/loop/pyforge-doctor. Folding the genuinely-lost shape back into 008 would rebuild exactly the ambiguity that cost an operator an hour and produced the wrong diagnosis "durability is broken".
- (constraint) Branch ABSENCE alone does not earn silence. The benign reading is "merged, therefore retired", so it is PROVEN: the story's own commit_sha (already carried on TaskPhaseSnapshot) must be reachable from the station branch via VcsPort.merge_base. Absent-and-unreachable, or absent-with-no-commit_sha, is retired-unmerged + MRS-SUPV-009 — a branch that vanished carrying unlanded work is real loss and must not inherit the quiet of the case it superficially resembles. The watcher's standing bias stays "one redundant finding over one missed loss".
- (decision) GitVcs.push is UNCHANGED. Raising VcsCommandError on "no such branch" is correct — its own docstring reasons that falling back would push to a target the caller never named, and that reasoning still holds. The defect was the CALLER asking for a push it did not need. Fixing the port would have traded a false alarm for a real hazard.
- (note) The test FakeVcs did not implement branch_exists even though VcsPort declares it — a non-conforming fake that surfaced as an AttributeError the moment production code used the real port surface. Fixed the fake rather than catching AttributeError in the supervisor: swallowing it there would mask exactly this class of bug. Worth remembering when adding any new VcsPort call.
- (change) S-1.11 SHIPPED, 2026-08-09 (FR-178). Reconciles by name: src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/ports/vcs.py and src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/adapters/vcs_git.py (new read-only `tracked_paths_matching`), src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/cli/init.py (`_shared_git_state_findings`, wired on preflight's MAIN path), src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/core/findings.py and src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/core/verdict.py (MRS-PREFLIGHT-013 in both registries), src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_init.py (4 tests + FakeVcs) and src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_findings.py (exact-contents guard). Also src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_upstream_cli.py — see below.
- (decision) The check asks "does this rule shadow TRACKED paths", NOT "is this rule on a list". A rule matching nothing tracked is ordinary local hygiene and stays silent; one matching tracked paths hides their future siblings invisibly. Self-validating against the repo as it actually is — no baseline to maintain, no vocabulary to keep current, and it cannot rot the way a declared list does. Proven live on first run: it found /.claude/skills shadowing 929 tracked paths AND a pre-existing /.claude/settings.json rule nobody had noticed.
- (note) CAUGHT ITSELF, and the catch is the point: these six files first appeared as non-gating [drift-presumed], not [drift], because this Spec's contract had already moved in #331 — so an unnamed change degrades to informational and `spec-surface-check` reported a clean OK. Green-because-unproven is exactly what S-13.2 made visible and what CAP-6 exists to end. Reconciled by naming rather than accepting the quiet green.
- (correction) test_upstream_cli.py::test_the_real_five_entry_register_content_round_trips pinned the upstream register to EXACTLY five entries; PR #333 added three real bmad-loop gaps and broke it. Renamed to ..._the_real_register_content_round_trips and relaxed to a subset assertion ("the founding five must survive every later addition") — a register that must not grow is not a register. It merged red because NO CI workflow runs any pyforge package suite; the marshal suite is local-only.
- (change) S-13.7 SHIPPED, 2026-08-09 (FR-174, CAP-7 of spec-surface-drift-reconciliation). Reconciles by name: src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/adapters/harness_bmadloop.py (`_SURFACE_RECONCILE_COMMAND`, appended in `render_policy_toml`) and src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_harness_policy_render.py (3 new tests + the exact-list assertions extended).
- (decision) APPENDED AT RENDER, deliberately NOT added as a policy layer. `verify_commands` composes last-wins across the four layers (AD-16), and all eight stations set their own — so a repo-wide default would be silently DROPPED by every one of them. Rendering appends, so a ninth station inherits the guard automatically and nobody has to remember to declare it in a ninth place (derive, don't declare). Proven by test: a project layer setting its own verify commands cannot drop the guard.
- (decision) The command is plain `python scripts/spec_surface_check.py`, not a pixi task. The detector is pure stdlib (git + hashlib), so it needs no environment, and it sidesteps the deep-worktree pixi path-length panic that breaks other gates inside bmad-loop run worktrees. NEVER `--write-baseline` — a producer that can stamp its own baseline is exactly the laundering S-13.2 exists to end; the loop must reconcile by NAMING the paths it changed. Pinned by its own test.
- (decision) A station with EMPTY verify_commands still gets the guard, so the loop cannot skip reconciliation by declaring no tests. This changed `test_empty_verify_commands_renders_empty_list`'s contract deliberately: "empty" was never the invariant worth protecting; "the surface is always reconciled" is.
- (note) Render is idempotent — `marshal config --write-harness-policy` runs on every preflight and home refresh, so the guard de-duplicates rather than accumulating. Pinned by test; without it bmad-loop would run the same check N times and grow policy.toml forever.
- (note) The eight loop homes carry ALREADY-RENDERED policy.toml files; they pick this up on their next `marshal config --write-harness-policy` (preflight/refresh). pyforge-doctor's live run at 20260808-210255-c88b was deliberately NOT re-rendered mid-run.
- (change) S-1.12 SHIPPED, 2026-08-09 (FR-180). Reconciles by name: src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/cli/init.py (`_home_currency_findings`, wired on preflight's main path, plus the `import subprocess` its own except clause needed), src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/core/findings.py and src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/core/verdict.py (MRS-PREFLIGHT-014 in both registries), src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_init.py (3 tests + FakeVcs gains resolve_ref/merge_base), src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_findings.py (exact-contents guard) and src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/meta/test_ad11_write_boundary.py (_RecordingVcs conformed).
- (decision) THE CHECK BELONGS AT PREFLIGHT, NOT THE LANDING. FR-173 (S-4.12) makes a LANDING leave the home current — but every landing on 2026-08-09 was done by hand with `gh pr merge`, so that code never ran and eight loop homes drifted 33-74 commits behind main with nothing reporting it. Preflight is the chokepoint every `factory spin` must pass; a promise attached to a bypassable path is not a guarantee.
- (decision) ERROR, not WARN, because the failure is SILENT. A home on an old baseline sees its own drift as non-gating [drift-presumed], `spec_surface_check` exits 0, and the S-13.7 guard never bites — measured the same day, a current home self-reconciled 4/4 while a stale one managed 0/3, with nothing failed and nothing logged. Three stories' worth of governance simply did not happen. It clears with one `git merge --ff-only`, so it can gate without ever being an unclearable red — the same argument that lets [drift-blind] gate.
- (constraint) AHEAD is not BEHIND. A home carrying unlanded story merges is the ordinary mid-run state; refusing it would block every spin of a station whose work has not landed, which is the opposite of the intent. Pinned by its own test.
- (note) FOUND WHILE WIRING IT: cli/init.py did not import `subprocess`, which the new except clause names — so the "never turn a probe into a refusal" safety net would itself have raised NameError on the first probe failure. Caught by the AD-11 write-boundary meta-test, not by review. A swallow-everything handler is only as good as its imports.
- (note) FR-173 is AMENDED by FR-180 to include the PUSH. Returning the station branch to main locally is not enough: provisioning reads ORIGIN, and seven homes sat 33-74 commits behind there after their work had already landed. Pushed at wind-down; the amendment is what stops it recurring.
- (correction) S-1.12 SHIPPED BROKEN IN #347 AND IS FIXED HERE (2026-08-09). Two defects, both invisible to its own passing tests. Reconciles by name: src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/cli/init.py, src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_init.py and src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/meta/test_ad11_write_boundary.py.
- (note) DEFECT 1 — `import subprocess` was never added, though `_home_currency_findings`'s except clause names it. Every marshal test passed because FakeVcs NEVER RAISES, so the except clause was never evaluated; against the real GitVcs, preflight crashed with NameError. A swallow-everything handler is only as good as its imports, and an untested exception path is not a safety net.
- (note) DEFECT 2 — the check called `resolve_ref(repo_root, "origin/main")` and `resolve_ref(home, "HEAD")`. `resolve_ref` prefixes refs/heads/, so it resolves BRANCH NAMES only: both raised VcsCommandError, which the function swallows by design, so the check silently did NOTHING while all three of its unit tests passed. The fake returned whatever it was told and never modelled the port's semantics. Now uses `worktree_head_sha(home)` and `resolve_ref(repo_root, "main")`.
- (decision) A REAL-GIT regression test was added (test_home_currency_uses_port_methods_that_actually_resolve): it runs the actual GitVcs against an actual repository, because a fake cannot catch a wrong-method bug — only the real port can. This is the second time tonight a fake hid a defect (the first: FakeVcs lacking branch_exists in S-1.11), and both times the tests were green while production was wrong.

## Surface reconcile 2026-08-09 — story 4-11 landing (land refuses while a run is in flight)

- (event) Seven governed paths moved when story `4-11-marshal-land-refuses-while-a-run-is-in-flight` landed; naming each rather than stamping blind. `core/status.py` ADDS the pure predicate `is_run_live(facts)` — the one fact `land` needs before honoring a policy-true `landing_branch_retirement`. It reads `FleetHomeFacts` DIRECTLY and deliberately never calls `derive_home_state`, because that function's `"idle"` collapses "no run ever" and "a live supervisor between stories" into one display string, and the second case is exactly what must still be caught. `core/findings.py` and `core/verdict.py` ADD the eighth `MRS-LAND-*` code, `MRS-LAND-008`, classified WARN — the merge still proceeds, only the branch's own retirement is skipped, for THIS invocation only and never as a policy write. `cli/land.py` wires the refusal and the `--retire-live-branch` override. `tests/unit/test_land.py` (+469 lines), `test_status.py` (+100) and `test_findings.py` cover the predicate's truth table and the downgrade path.
- (constraint) `is_run_live` treats `journal_unreadable` as LIVE. That is the conservative direction on purpose, mirroring `core/retire.py`'s existing precedent that a fact which cannot be proven is refused rather than defaulted to delete. A liveness check that guesses "dead" when it cannot read the journal would reintroduce the exact hazard the story exists to close.
- (event) The motivating incident is recorded in the docstring: a live 9-story run was nearly landed over, "avoided only because a human read the source first". Landing this story converts that human vigilance into a machine refusal — the standing operator instruction to never run `marshal land` against a live station now has code behind it.
- (event) Story landed 2026-08-09 after bmad-loop recorded it `failed`. The dev pass had completed; the session hung on `running stop hook` at 7m38s and was timeout-killed, and the recorded failure reason was `verify command failed (rc=1): python scripts/spec_surface_check.py` — i.e. this very memlog not having moved. Re-verified on the landing branch: `pyforge-marshal-test` 3077 passed, `pyforge-deps-test` 60 passed. Baseline scoped-stamped to this spec and to `pyforge-doctor/spec-pyforge-doctor` only.

## Surface reconcile 2026-08-10 — story 4-12 landing (a landing leaves the loop home current with main)

- (event) Eight governed paths moved when story `4-12-a-landing-leaves-the-loop-home-current-with-main` landed (commit `12e0a00732`); naming each rather than stamping blind. `ports/vcs.py` and `adapters/vcs_git.py` ADD two new `VcsPort` primitives: `fetch` (network, updates only `refs/remotes/origin/<base>`) and `fast_forward` (`git merge --ff-only`, never a forced merge/`--no-ff`/rebase/`reset --hard`). `cli/land.py` ADDS `_resync_home_branch`, wired into ALL THREE of `run_land`'s wave-outcome exits (the already-landed shortcut, the full-merge path, and the `if not wave_keys` no-op — this story's own named primary scenario, between-runs drift accumulating unobserved) and sets `data["home_current"]`, plus a rendered `_render_text_land` line when the key is present. `core/findings.py` and `core/verdict.py` ADD the NINTH `MRS-LAND-*` code, `MRS-LAND-009`, classified WARN — deliberately not a reuse of `MRS-LAND-008` (that code is Story 4.11's live-run branch-retirement refusal, a different failure entirely, not yet merged into this codebase when 4.12 was authored). `tests/unit/test_vcs_git.py` (+137, real-git fixtures) and `tests/unit/test_land.py` (+250) cover the two new primitives and the resync helper's full I/O matrix; `tests/unit/test_findings.py` extends the exact-contents registry guard.
- (constraint) Resync is restricted to `landing_merge_strategy == "merge"` — under `"squash"`/`"rebase"` the landed commits are never ancestors of `origin/<base>`, so `fast_forward` would fail on EVERY invocation, permanently and by construction; a WARN that can never clear is noise, not signal, so `home_current` stays absent under those two strategies, byte-identical in shape to `resync_enabled=False`. This was review pass 1's `[high][intent_gap]` finding (2026-08-09) — the first draft's Boundaries never addressed `landing_merge_strategy` at all — resolved by the operator restricting scope rather than reusing `VcsPort.is_branch_merged`'s squash-aware proof, which would have been a materially riskier operation than a fast-forward.
- (event) Review pass 2 (2026-08-10) added an identity guard before any fetch/fast-forward is attempted: `VcsPort.worktree_head_sha(home)` vs `VcsPort.resolve_ref(git_repo_root, head_branch)`, the SAME pair `MRS-DEPLOY-017` already uses in the full-merge path. Without it, a `home` that had drifted onto a different ref (or a detached HEAD) would get THAT ref silently advanced while `head_branch` stayed stale and `home_current` still reported `True`. A mismatch now reports the same `MRS-LAND-009` WARN shape as any other resync failure, no fast-forward attempted. The same pass also split `fetch` and `fast_forward` into separate `try` blocks so the WARN names the step that actually failed, rather than always saying "could not fast-forward" when the real failure was the network fetch.
- (note) Story landed 2026-08-09/10 after bmad-loop recorded the dev pass complete, then failed deterministic verification on this SAME check — `python scripts/spec_surface_check.py` reporting these eight paths drifted against this memlog — the identical failure mode `S-4.11`'s own landing hit one entry above. Re-verified on the landing branch: `pyforge-marshal-test` 3077+ passed. Baseline scoped-stamped to this spec only.
- (event) Review pass 3 (2026-08-10), run as part of the same repair session that reconciled the drift above, touched `cli/land.py` and `tests/unit/test_land.py` again (already named, no new path added to the governed set). Split the identity guard's combined `try` into two (mirroring the fetch/fast_forward split pass 2 already made) so the WARN names which of `resolve_ref`/`worktree_head_sha` actually failed, fixed the same unquoted-`origin/{base}` bug pass 2 fixed for the fast-forward message, added test coverage for both identity-guard exception paths and for an identity mismatch reached through the already-landed shortcut specifically (previously only its success path was tested), added a `format="text"` test for `_render_text_land`'s new line, and corrected this spec's own Code Map (missing the `merge_strategy` parameter) and two docstrings that overclaimed the identity guard's provenance was uniform across all three call sites. 3097 tests pass (was 3093 after pass 2, +4 from this pass).

## Surface reconcile 2026-08-10 — story 4-13 landing (the loop's deferred work reaches the tracked ledger)

- (event) Seven governed paths moved when story `4-13-the-loops-deferred-work-reaches-the-tracked-ledger` landed (commit `bb2a7a95c0`); naming each rather than stamping blind. `core/deferred_work.py` is NEW: the pure Tier-3 review-budget-followup promotion core (`DeferralCandidate`, `parse_followup_deferrals`, `promoted_id`, `render_ledger_entry`, `deferrals_to_promote`), mirroring `core/promotion.py`'s own pure/impure split (AD-4) — no I/O, no `pathlib`, no subprocess, no clock. `cli/land.py` ADDS `_promote_deferred_work`, wired into BOTH of `run_land`'s landed-confirmation exits (the already-landed shortcut and immediately after `forge.merge_pr` succeeds), plus the `_render_text_land` line for `deferred_work_promoted` when present. `core/findings.py` and `core/verdict.py` ADD the TENTH `MRS-LAND-*` code, `MRS-LAND-010`, classified WARN — fires only on the tracked ledger's own advisory-lock contention or a `VcsPort.commit_paths` failure, never on the ordinary nothing-to-promote case. `tests/unit/test_deferred_work.py` (NEW, 286 lines) covers the pure core incl. the `DW-6` interleaved-anonymous-bullet parse shape and the prefix-collision idempotency regression; `tests/unit/test_land.py` (+473) covers promote-on-merge, promote-on-already-landed, idempotent re-run, lock-contention WARN, no-match no-op, multi-candidate promotion, and the missing/deleted-ledger bootstrap and concurrent-deletion cases; `tests/unit/test_findings.py` extends the exact-contents registry guard.
- (decision) The promoted id stays `DW-FU-<epic>-<seq>` with no numeric counter, deviating from the epics' literal `DW-<story>-<n>` phrasing — the convention already live across every prior promotion in this ledger (`DW-FU-1-1` .. `DW-FU-3-5`) and in warden's/doctor's own ledgers, since at most one `review-budget-followup` entry has ever existed per story.
- (decision) Idempotency is a boundary-aware TOKEN match (`core.deferred_work._tracked_promoted_ids`), never a bare substring test — a 2026-08-10 review finding: `"DW-FU-1-1" in text` false-positives the moment the text also carries `"DW-FU-1-10"`, a real collision already live in this repo (story 1.10 alongside the already-promoted `DW-FU-1-1`; epic 4 now runs 4-1..4-13 too).
- (note) Story landed 2026-08-09/10 after bmad-loop recorded the dev pass complete, then failed deterministic verification on this SAME check — `python scripts/spec_surface_check.py` reporting these seven paths drifted against this memlog — the identical failure mode `S-4.11`'s and `S-4.12`'s own landings hit in the two entries above. Re-verified on the landing branch: `pyforge-marshal-test` 3131 passed (9 slow deselected), `pyforge-deps-test` 67 passed, `lint-imports` clean (3 contracts kept, 0 broken), `deferred_work_check.py` unchanged ("OK: every Tier-3 deferral has a tracked twin"). Baseline scoped-stamped to this spec only.

## Surface reconcile 2026-08-10 — story 4-14 re-derivation, iteration 1 (the failed-story safety net is reported)

- (event) Six governed paths moved when story `4-14-the-failed-story-safety-net-is-reported` (FR-176) was RE-DERIVED after its first implementation (`7e96393a05`) was reverted (`8410edba56`) over one high-severity review finding; naming each rather than stamping blind, reconciled in the SAME dev pass. Reconciles by name: src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/core/status.py (ADDS `failed_patches: tuple[dict[str, object], ...] = ()` to `FleetHomeFacts` and threads it into all three `build_fleet_row` row shapes — an independent filesystem signal, never hardcoded away in a degraded row, mirroring `unpushed_work`'s own established precedent; each entry now carries a `confidence` drawn from this SAME module's existing `CONFIDENCE_CONFIRMED`/`CONFIDENCE_UNCONFIRMED` constants, never a third vocabulary), src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/cli/status.py (ADDS `_gather_failed_patches`, a bare `Path.glob(".bmad-loop/runs/*/failed/*/changes.patch")` per home with no new `FsPort` primitive, mirroring `cli/spin.py::_latest_run_dir`'s own documented precedent, and `_merged_keys_for_slug`, the ONE shared policy-read-then-`promotion.merged_story_keys` sequence called by BOTH the new fleet fold and `_reconcile_ledger` — one owner for the merge-subject form, never a second classifier — wired into `run_status`'s per-home loop with a lazy, sweep-wide-cached `main` commit-subjects read, plus a tri-state `FAILED_PATCHES n= pending= unknown=` line in `_render_text_status`), src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/core/findings.py and src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/core/verdict.py (ADD two more `MRS-STATUS-*` codes, both WARN: `MRS-STATUS-010` for a failed-story patch with no CONFIRMING durable merge on `main`, including one whose own story-dir name doesn't even parse as a story key, and `MRS-STATUS-011` for a landed-status that could not be determined at all — documented at all three doc sites with BOTH of its causes), src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_status.py (adds `TestFailedPatches`, 14 cases, plus one `TestReconcileLedgerCli` regression guard and the extended `TestBuildFleetRow` idle-row equality assertion) and src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_findings.py (extends the exact-contents registry guard).
- (decision) "Landed" is git's durable-merge evidence only (AD-33), never the harness's own `state.json`/journal — a killed run's own journal never shows `done`, even after the story lands via a LATER successful run, so the journal was never a candidate signal here.
- (correction) Review pass 2 (2026-08-10) reverted the first implementation on ONE high-severity finding: `MRS-STATUS-010` asserted "has not landed" as established fact, derived from the NEGATIVE direction of `core.promotion.merged_story_keys` — the exact direction this package's own `core/status.py` already documents as proving nothing (`CONFIDENCE_UNCONFIRMED`, the 2026-08-07 code review; `required` in `schemas/status.json`; "5 rows for genuinely-merged stories, zero true positives"). Verified live against the real 12-patch fleet: of 3 WARNs, **2 were false** — `pyforge-marshal` 1.6 (landed as the squash subject `marshal: recover Story 1.6 … (#163)`, and named *in that very constant block* as one of the 5 known false positives) and `pyforge-steward` 8.1 (landed as `…from rxm7706/land/steward-8-1`; 22 such `land/<station>-<epic>-<seq>` merges exist on `main` and `normalize` anchors at position 0, so the leading station token makes the segment unparseable). Fixed by reusing this module's OWN confidence vocabulary on every entry (`done: true` → `confirmed`; `done: false` and `done: null` → `unconfirmed`) and by rewriting the finding message to state the UNCONFIRMED direction and WHY (naming both blind spots) instead of a definitional negative dressed as a fact about the world. The classifier's own blind spots are NOT fixed here — extending `merged_story_keys` would change three other already-shipped views (`_reconcile_ledger`, `cli/deploy.py`, `cli/land.py`) that consume the same single owner, so that is a separate story against `core/promotion.py`.
- (correction) Review pass 2 also fixed five lower-severity spec/implementation defects: (1) `--format text` collapsed the `done` tri-state, so an all-`null` sweep rendered `pending=0`, byte-identical to all-landed — the renderer now emits `pending=` AND `unknown=`; (2) `MRS-STATUS-011` had been overloaded in pass 1 with a per-slug policy-ERROR cause while all three doc sites still described only the unreadable-`main` cause and still claimed "at most ONCE for the whole sweep" — false for the per-home arm, which can fire once per misconfigured station, and its emitted message wrongly claimed "every patch found this sweep" degrades when only that one slug does; (3) the `is_file()` guard ran too late, so a directory literally named `changes.patch` still opened the `if patch_paths:` gate, paying for the git read and able to emit an ORPHANED `MRS-STATUS-011` while every row reported `failed_patches: []` — the filter now lives inside `_gather_failed_patches`; (4) a zero-byte `changes.patch` (a kill before any diff was written) produced a WARN pointing an operator at an empty file — now skipped entirely; (5) `MRS-STATUS-010` omitted `run_id` though the row entry carried it, so repeat failed attempts at one story across runs were distinguishable only by a long absolute path (live: `pyforge-steward` carries three run dirs).
- (correction) Pass 1's `_reconcile_ledger` refactor was a real behavior regression, and the re-derivation does NOT repeat it: pass 1 moved that view's policy read to AFTER its `git log` read, so a git failure returned before `_merged_keys_for_slug` was ever called and the `MRS-POLICY-004` diagnostic that pre-existing shipped view reports today was silently dropped. `_merged_keys_for_slug` is still the ONE shared sequence (KEEP), but the git-failure path now calls it too — discarding the meaningless `merged_keys` and keeping the findings — so no shipped diagnostic is lost. Guarded by a new `TestReconcileLedgerCli` regression test.
- (correction) `--reconcile-ledger`'s own docs claimed its `git log`-scale walk over `main` is "never folded into the default view" — this story folds exactly that half in, so both doc sites (the argparse help and the section comment) now say so, and the "most homes carry no failed patches" laziness rationale is deleted as measurably false: 7 of 8 live homes carry at least one patch (measured 2026-08-10). The caching is kept — reading `main` once per invocation and reusing it for every home is what bounds the cost — but laziness is now stated as a degenerate-case optimization for a genuinely patch-free fleet, never the common path.
- (note) MANDATORY live-fleet check (review pass 2 added it precisely because pass 1's all-synthetic fixtures passed while the feature was wrong on 2 of 3 real WARNs), run against the 8 real loop homes under `~/.bmad-loops/` with the worktree's own source on `PYTHONPATH`: **12 patches across 7 homes** (`pyforge-scribe` has none), sizes 26,363 B (`pyforge-mason` 1.3) to 10,218,067 B (`pyforge-atlas` 12.2). 3 `MRS-STATUS-010` WARNs — `pyforge-doctor` 6.9 (the one genuine unlanded patch), `pyforge-marshal` 1.6 and `pyforge-steward` 8.1 — every one of them now reported as UNCONFIRMED with `confidence: "unconfirmed"` and the reason named, never as established fact; every `done: true` entry carries `confidence: "confirmed"`. Verdict `warn`, exit 0 (the WARN-only Boundary holds). With `main` deliberately unreadable, all 12 degrade to `done: null` under exactly ONE `MRS-STATUS-011`, and `--format text` renders a non-zero `unknown=` per row (e.g. `FAILED_PATCHES n=3 pending=0 unknown=3`), distinguishable from all-landed. `--project pyforge-doctor` scoped the scan to that one home. `--reconcile-ledger --project pyforge-marshal` still reports its 22 discrepancies unchanged.
- (note) Verified on this branch: `pyforge-marshal-test` 3146 passed (9 slow deselected, +15 over the 3131 baseline), `pyforge-deps-test` 67 passed, `lint-imports` clean (3 contracts kept, 0 broken), `python scripts/spec_surface_check.py` reconciled. Baseline scoped-stamped to this spec only.
- (correction) Review pass 3 (2026-08-10) patched three real defects in the re-derivation itself, no revert needed. (1) The pass-2 zero-byte skip and the `patch.stat()` `OSError` skip both ran INSIDE the caller's per-entry loop, i.e. after the `if patch_paths:` gate had already opened — reintroducing the very orphaned-`MRS-STATUS-011` hole the relocated `is_file()` filter was meant to close. Reproduced live: a home whose only match is a zero-byte `changes.patch`, with `main` unreadable, emitted `MRS-STATUS-011` while every row reported `failed_patches: []` and the verdict flipped `clean → warn` with nothing to show. `_gather_failed_patches` now returns `(path, size_bytes)` pairs and owns ALL THREE reportability tests (`is_file()`, `st_size > 0`, `stat()`-raises), so a non-empty return genuinely means "there is something to report" and the caller never re-`stat()`s. (2) `MRS-STATUS-011` named no patch at all, while the intent contract's Always bullet requires a patch whose landed-status "could not be determined" to raise "exactly one WARN naming it" — an operator got a count and no story keys for up to 12 unresolved patches. Both arms now name their patches (`_name_patches`), which required moving the sweep-wide arm's emission to AFTER the per-home loop, since the homes scanned later are not yet known at the point the git read fails; still exactly ONE WARN for that arm, and the read is still attempted at most once. (3) A fourth inline copy of the best-effort story-key renderer — `core/status.py::_render_story_key_best_effort` already is that function, and `cli` may import `core` (only the reverse is AD-3/AD-4-forbidden) — now reused, the same "one owner" correction pass 1 applied to `_merged_keys_for_slug`.
- (decision) The exit-code guard on `_merged_keys_for_slug`'s findings now tests `core.verdict.classify(f.code)`, not `Finding.severity`. The Boundary being defended is about this command's EXIT CODE, which `_CLASSIFY_TABLE` decides; severity is only correlated with it today (every `Verdict.UNEVALUABLE` policy code happens to be constructed at `Severity.ERROR`), so a future UNEVALUABLE-but-WARN policy code would have slipped through and changed `marshal status`'s exit code. The same branch also no longer discards non-blocking policy findings wholesale — an unrelated WARN accompanying an ERROR used to vanish.
- (note) Two pass-3 findings were deliberately NOT fixed and are recorded in the deferred-work ledger instead. `Path.glob` silently yields a PARTIAL result when an intermediate directory is unreadable (verified against CPython 3.14.6: a `chmod 000` story dir makes its patch vanish with no exception and no finding), so a real unlanded patch can go unreported — closing it honestly needs enumeration-integrity reporting under a new finding code, and the trigger requires broken permissions inside a loop home that the harness itself creates. And a patch for the story a home is re-driving RIGHT NOW warns as though abandoned; the row already carries `current_story`, but suppressing would contradict the Always bullet and annotating needs a contract decision about the entry shape. `core/status.py`'s `failed_patches` docstring no longer claims this field has "no could-not-be-consulted case of its own" — the caller's own `except OSError: return ()` and the glob-truncation limit both contradicted it.
- (note) Re-verified after review pass 3: `pyforge-marshal-test` 3149 passed (9 slow deselected, +3 from the pass: only-unreportable-patches-never-orphans-a-finding, unreadable-main-WARN-names-every-degraded-patch, journal-unreadable-row-still-reports-failed-patches), `pyforge-deps-test` 67 passed, `lint-imports` clean (3 contracts kept, 0 broken), `ruff check` clean on `cli/status.py`. Live-fleet re-check unchanged: 12 patches across 7 homes, pending keys `1.6`/`6.9`/`8.1`, every non-landed entry `confidence: "unconfirmed"`, no message asserting "has not landed", verdict `warn`, exit 0. Baseline re-stamped, scoped to this spec only.

- (correction) Review pass 4 (2026-08-10) patched six defects in story `4-14`'s re-derivation, no revert needed; three governed paths moved (`src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/cli/status.py`, `src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/core/status.py`, `src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_status.py`). (1) Three doc sites still asserted that a POSITIVE `merged_story_keys` match "is proof", and the pass stamped that reading onto the wire as `confidence: "confirmed"` — while this project's OWN ledger records the positive direction as cross-project contaminated. Re-verified live against `main`'s 2,353 subjects: `pyforge-mason`, `pyforge-doctor` and `pyforge-scribe` each resolve ~30 keys, most belonging to OTHER stations (mason returns marshal's `4.1`–`4.10`), so a future killed mason story 4.1 would report `done: true` and fire no WARN — the SILENCING direction, the one with data-loss consequences, while all three prior passes hardened only the noisy negative one. The `confidence` VALUE is unchanged (the Code Map mandates it, and `reconcile_ledger_vs_git` ships the same vocabulary for the same evidence), but all three sites now say "the stronger direction", name the contamination, and point at the deferral — the same overclaim-correction pass 3 applied to `FleetHomeFacts.failed_patches`'s own `()` claim, applied consistently this time. (2) `MRS-STATUS-010`'s message interpolated `story_key` AND `path` unsanitized while `_render_text_status`'s findings block prints one finding per line without escaping — reproduced live: a story dir named `4-11-fine\n  MRS-STATUS-999 [error] INJECTED` forged a findings line no finding emitted. A `_one_line` helper (hoisted from the sanitization `_name_patches` already did in the SAME file) now covers both; the wider `MRS-STATUS-008` instance of this class stays deferred. (3) The per-slug `MRS-STATUS-011` asserted "cannot resolve this project's own merge-subject policy", false for most codes reaching it: `MRS-POLICY-001` (an unrecognized key) classifies `UNEVALUABLE` and so blocks, yet `compose` still returns a usable `merge_subject_template`. The message now states what happened, names the WITHHELD codes (previously invisible in the only view most sweeps run), and points at `--reconcile-ledger` for the finding itself. (4) `_name_patches` rendered BARE story keys, and story numbers repeat across stations by construction — live, `pyforge-doctor` and `pyforge-warden` both carry a `6-9-*` patch, so the one sweep-wide WARN read `(6.9, 6.9)` and located neither; now `<slug>/<story_key>`. (5) This module's docstring claimed "No `sprint-status.yaml`, ledger, or any other hand-maintained feed is ever read for the fleet summary" — false since this story, which reads each patch-carrying home's `marshal-policy.toml`; corrected with the AD-5 reasoning that still holds (it is CONFIGURATION, never a story-state claim). (6) KEEP instruction #3 — "`main` is read at most ONCE per sweep", asserted at four doc sites — had zero coverage: `_FakeVcs` recorded no call count, so deleting the guard left the suite green while turning one `git log` per invocation into one per home. Now counted, with tests for both the once-only and the never-when-empty halves.
- (note) Four pass-4 findings were deliberately NOT fixed and are recorded in the deferred-work ledger instead: durability reads LOCAL `main` only while `cli/deploy.py` — the same shared owner's other consumer — reads `origin/main` + `main` (live reflog shows 22–34 min fetch-vs-fast-forward windows, so a just-landed story warns as pending), bounded by the frozen intent contract's own "for `main`" and consistent with `_reconcile_ledger` in this same module; `core.promotion.count_conforming_subjects` is unused, so a `main` yielding ZERO conforming subjects (shallow clone, grafted history) is indistinguishable from N genuinely-unlanded stories, the exact ambiguity `cli/deploy.py` reports as `subjects_examined`/`subjects_matched`; the `blocking` guard degrades a whole home on ANY non-CLEAN/WARN policy finding, discarding an already-correct `merged_keys` including proof-grade positives (conservative and safe-direction, but over-broad); and `--escalations` emits `MRS-STATUS-010` for rows the filter then removes — the pre-existing `MRS-STATUS-008` pattern, but newly reachable at scale (up to 12 orphan findings against the sibling's current zero).
- (note) Re-verified after review pass 4: `pyforge-marshal-test` **3154 passed** (9 slow deselected, +5 from the pass), `pyforge-deps-test` 67 passed, `lint-imports` clean (3 contracts kept, 0 broken), `ruff check` clean on both changed source files (the 2 remaining errors in `test_status.py` are pre-existing — verified by running ruff against HEAD's own copy of that file). Live-fleet re-check unchanged, as expected for a message/doc/test-only pass: 12 patches across 7 homes, 3 `MRS-STATUS-010` (`1.6`/`6.9`/`8.1`), 9 `confirmed` + 3 `unconfirmed`, verdict `warn`, exit 0. Baseline re-stamped, scoped to this spec only.

- (correction) Review pass 5 (2026-08-10) patched six defects in story `4-14`, no revert; three governed paths moved (`src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/cli/status.py`, `core/findings.py`, `core/verdict.py`) plus the test module. (1) The sweep-wide `MRS-STATUS-011` interpolated the raw `VcsCommandError` with NO `_one_line` — the exact injection class pass 4 added `_one_line` to close, applied to `MRS-STATUS-010`'s operands and missed on the one arm that interpolates git's OWN stderr. This one needs no adversarial filename: real git, asked for a `main` that does not exist locally, answers in THREE lines, so a single WARN printed as three text lines (`--format text` is the default), two of them starting with git-controlled text and no `MRS-` prefix — and an unreadable `main` is precisely the condition the code exists to report. (2) `_name_patches` closed pass 4's CROSS-HOME key collision and left the CROSS-RUN one open: a home accumulates one `failed/<story>/` per killed attempt, so repeated attempts at one story in one home rendered `(steward/8.1, steward/8.1, steward/8.1)` — live, `pyforge-steward` carries three run dirs — and `MRS-STATUS-011` is the ONLY report a `done: null` patch ever gets, since `010` fires solely for `done is False`. Now `<slug>/<story_key>@<run_id>`, matching the `run_id` `MRS-STATUS-010` already carries for the same reason (pass 2). (3) `_one_line` collapsed only `\n`/`\r`, too narrow for its own stated threat model — `\v`, `\f`, `\x1c`–`\x1e`, `\x85`, `\u2028`, `\u2029` are all legal in a POSIX/UTF-8 filename and all split by `str.splitlines`, the very method this story's own forged-line test asserts with; widened to every C0 control, `\x7f`, and the three non-C0 breaks. (4) `_gather_failed_patches`'s docstring claimed an unreadable `.bmad-loop` tree reaches its `except OSError` and "degrades to none found", contradicting `core/status.py::FleetHomeFacts.failed_patches`, which pass 3 corrected in this same story; re-verified on CPython 3.14.6 that `Path.glob` suppresses the error and truncates silently, so the `except` guards only a pre-iteration failure.
