## Surface reconcile 2026-08-13 — story 8-2 landing (region parser — span discovery, nesting
rejection, fence awareness)

- (event) Two governed paths moved when story
  `8-2-region-parser-span-discovery-nesting-rejection-fence-awareness` landed (commit
  `accc097e6a`), the first whole-file scan over story 8-1's per-line marker grammar -- the span
  S-8.3's substitution will operate on.
  `src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/regions/parse.py` ADDS
  `RegionParseError(PyforgeError, ValueError)`, the frozen `RegionSpan` dataclass (name,
  model-version, declared sha, body/begin/end byte-spans), a private fence-toggle pair
  (`_fence_delimiter`/`_closes_fence`, CommonMark-shaped, html-format only), and
  `parse_regions(text, fmt) -> tuple[RegionSpan, ...]`: walks the text tracking a running byte
  offset and at most one open region, raising `RegionParseError` for nesting/overlap (one check
  for both), an unterminated begin, a duplicate region name, and an end-marker mismatch (including
  a stray end with nothing open); skips fenced lines entirely when `fmt is RegionFormat.HTML`.
  `src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_seed_regions_parse.py` ADDS full I/O &
  Edge-Case Matrix coverage (zero/one/two regions, nesting/overlap, unterminated begin, duplicate
  name, mismatched/stray end, fence awareness incl. CommonMark opener/closer edge cases, CRLF/LF/
  mixed-terminator parity, multi-byte UTF-8 body-span byte-offset proof, zero-length body, eager
  `fmt` validation on empty text, and `MarkerError`/`NotImplementedError` propagation), plus three
  step-04 review-driven regression tests (below). This spec's own broad package-path glob covers
  both; no narrower sibling spec claims either.
- (note) Review pass: 2 patches applied -- a high-consequence fix (confirmed by direct execution)
  where an unclosed fence's end-of-text error silently dropped the fact that an entirely unrelated
  region, opened and never closed before the fence ever appeared, was also still open; the raised
  `RegionParseError` now names both when both are true. Plus one low-severity coverage addition (a
  second, later-opened fence left unclosed, proving `fence_lineno` tracks the CURRENT fence, not
  stale state from an earlier closed one). Two findings were re-discoveries of this spec's own
  first review pass's already-rejected findings (the `splitlines()` line-boundary breadth beyond
  CRLF/LF/CR, and the untested nesting-vs-duplicate-name raise-order precedence) -- rejected again
  for the same recorded reasoning, which still holds. A `parse_marker_line` return-type dispatch
  finding (no explicit `isinstance(EndMarker)` guard) was rejected as a false positive: `markers.py`
  declares `-> BeginMarker | EndMarker | None`, a closed union pyright already proves exhaustive.
  Remaining findings (fence-awareness scoped to html-only, two micro-optimizations, three further
  coverage-only gaps, error messages carrying no file identity) rejected as either explicitly
  spec-mandated scope or no plausible consequence within this story's boundaries -- full rationale

  shape this repo's own root `.gitignore` uses in `!src/**/packages/*/**`) compiled to a regex
  requiring a literal trailing `/`, which no walked file path carries -- confirmed live to match
  nothing at all. `_translate_pattern_to_regex` now special-cases a trailing `**` segment as an
  unconditional `.*` rather than the optional `(?:.*/)?` a leading/middle `**` uses. (5) Anchoring
  checked only for a LEADING `/`; real gitignore anchors on ANY `/` before the pattern's end --
  confirmed live that this repo's own `.idea/**/workspace.xml` pattern incorrectly matched at any
  depth (e.g. `sub/.idea/workspace.xml`). Fixed to `anchored = "/" in pattern_body`. Both
  reviewers independently found the SLASHSTAR, unreadable-file, and trailing-`**` bugs; Blind

- (note) Follow-up review pass 2026-08-13 (`followup_review_recommended: true` from the prior
  pass): Blind Hunter and Edge Case Hunter run independently again (no shared context, no memory
  of the prior pass), both against the diff since `95f1db76aa` (this story's baseline, which now
  includes all 8 prior-pass patches). 5 patches applied this pass, both false leads verified
  against actual invariants before being ruled out: (1) Edge Case Hunter flagged `entry.format is
  None` reaching `_classify_hybrid`'s `assert` as a crash vector -- verified against
  `model/manifest.py:331-332`, `ManifestEntry.__post_init__` unconditionally raises `ValueError`
  for any hybrid-managed-region entry with a falsy `format`, at every construction site; the
  assert is the same established type-narrowing convention already used at `cli/init.py:887` for
  an analogous guaranteed invariant -- REJECTED, unreachable. (2) Blind Hunter flagged
  `_classify_hybrid` on an entry with zero declared `regions` as vacuously `present-conformant`
  regardless of file content -- verified against `model/manifest.py:345-346`,
  `ManifestEntry.__post_init__` requires at least one region for every hybrid-managed-region
  entry -- REJECTED, unreachable. (3) `_translate_segment_to_regex`'s blanket `re.escape` never
  translated a gitignore bracket expression (`[abc]`, `[a-z]`, `[!abc]`/`[^abc]` negated) into a
  regex character class -- CONFIRMED LIVE against this repo's own root `.gitignore`, which uses
  bracket expressions extensively (`*.py[cod]`, `[Dd]ebug/`, `[Ww][Ii][Nn]32/`, dozens more):
  `_translate_pattern_to_regex('*.py[cod]', anchored=False).fullmatch('foo.pyc')` returned
  `False` before the fix -- every one of those exclusions silently matched nothing at all, so
  `.pyc`/`Debug/`/`bin/`/`obj/`-shaped paths from THIS repo's own tree would leak into
  `Inventory.tree` uncaught. Rewrote `_translate_segment_to_regex` as a character-by-character
  translator with bracket-class support (unterminated `[` falls back to a literal, matching real
  gitignore's own tolerance); re-verified live against this repo's actual `.gitignore` after the
  fix (`*.pyc`/`bin`/`Debug` all correctly excluded). Both reviewers independently reasoned about
  the hybrid-entry invariants (both false leads); only Blind Hunter found the redundant-resolve
  inefficiency; only Edge Case Hunter found the bracket-class gap and the `os.walk`
  permission-denied silent-skip observation (the latter rejected: `os.walk`'s default `onerror`
  behavior already matches this module's own established graceful-degradation posture for every
  other unreadable input, e.g. `_load_gitignore_rules`, and nothing downstream consumes
  `Inventory.tree` yet to be affected by it). (4) `_resolve_within_repo` re-resolved
  `repo_root.resolve()` on every manifest entry instead of once per `classify()` call -- real,
  low-severity redundant filesystem work against the module's own "walk once, cache it" ethos;
  hoisted to `classify()`, threaded through as `resolved_root`. (5) Three low-severity coverage
  gaps folded in as new tests: a whole-file class (`copied-managed`) pointed at a directory is
  `present-conformant` (the module docstring's own claim, previously unproven for the non-hybrid
  case); a symlink inside `repo_root` pointing outside it hits the same `_resolve_within_repo`
  containment guard as a hand-crafted absolute/`../` path (a more realistic real-world escape
  vector, already correctly handled by `Path.resolve()`'s own symlink-following, but untested); a
  `.gitignore` containing only comments/blank lines degrades to "nothing excluded" (a different
  code branch than a missing `.gitignore` -- the read succeeds but every line is filtered out).
  10 findings rejected this pass: the 2 unreachable-invariant leads above, the `os.walk`
  permission-denied observation, `PRESENT_LEGACY`'s intentional dead-call-site status (already
  justified in-module, matches `FindingType`'s own precedent), 2 findings about the diff's own
  documentation/memlog prose (not code defects), and 4 gitignore-grammar edge cases (backslash-
  escaped `#`/`!`/mid-pattern, a malformed `foo**bar` double-star segment which real git itself
  declares undefined, leading-whitespace-only trimming, a double-leading-slash `//foo` pattern) --
  none proven against this repo's actual `.gitignore` (grepped for all four shapes: zero matches),
  so ruled unproven/hypothetical against a hand-rolled matcher the module's own docstring already
  scopes as a bounded "subset," not full gitwildmatch fidelity. Re-verified after all patches:
  `pyforge-marshal-test` 3741 passed (9 slow deselected, up from 3736 pre-pass), `ruff check`
  clean on both files (`All checks passed!`; package-wide count unchanged at 186 pre-existing),
  `pyright` unchanged (`inventory.py` zero, test file's 5 errors the same pre-existing
  `reportMissingImports` category, package total 592 same as pre-pass), `lint-imports --config

## Surface reconcile 2026-08-13 — story 9-3 landing (content hashing for managed files and
regions)

- (event) Three governed paths moved when story `9-3-content-hashing-for-managed-files-and-regions`
  (FR-106/FR-86/P-07) landed:
  `src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/detect/hashes.py` (NEW --
  `normalize_line_endings` (CRLF and a lone CR both collapse to LF) + `hash_content` (sha256 over
  the normalized UTF-8 bytes, truncated to 8 lowercase hex characters -- the one hash algorithm
  both whole-file and region-body hashing share, matching `regions.markers.region_sha`'s own
  output shape without delegating to it, since `region_sha` hashes raw with no normalization);
  `region_body_text(text, span)` (the `span.body_span` UTF-8 byte-slice, the identical operation
  S-8.3's substitution performs); `check_managed_file`/`check_managed_region` (`Finding | None`,
  `None` only when a non-`None` `recorded_sha` matches the computed hash, a HARD
  `managed-file-modified`/`managed-region-modified` `Finding.new(...)` for any mismatch INCLUDING
  `recorded_sha is None` -- FR-86's "not silently accepted" adopted-out-of-band case). Zero I/O:
  every function takes already-read `str`/`RegionSpan`/`recorded_sha`; no `.marshal/seed-state.yml`
  read, no `ArtifactState`/`Classification` construction, no `inventory.py` import.
  `src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_seed_detect_hashes.py` (NEW -- full I/O &
  Edge-Case Matrix coverage: file/region hash match and mismatch, the `recorded_sha is None`
  out-of-band case for both, CRLF/LF hash parity, region hashing unaffected by content outside the
  region, an empty region body, multi-byte UTF-8 near marker boundaries -- region-bearing cases
  build real `RegionSpan`s via `regions.parse.parse_regions`, mirroring
  `test_seed_regions_parse.py`'s own `_begin`/`_end`/`_doc` helpers).
  `src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/meta/test_p07_no_hash_comparison_in_apply.py` (NEW --
  P-07 AST-scan meta-test asserting `seed/apply/` never imports `detect.hashes` (any absolute or
  relative import shape) or `hashlib` directly, self-tested against 7 synthetic-violation and 3
  synthetic-non-violation cases, mirroring `test_ad19_no_adapter_branch.py`'s own
  scan-plus-self-test technique). This Spec's own broad package-path glob covers all three; no
  narrower sibling spec claims any.

## Surface reconcile 2026-08-13 — story 9-3 review pass (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter)

- (event) Two governed paths changed again, patching review findings from an independent
  Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter pass (no shared context between reviewers) over story
  `9-3-content-hashing-for-managed-files-and-regions`:
  `src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/detect/hashes.py` -- extracted a
  shared `_hash_mismatch_detail(current_sha, recorded_sha)` helper so `check_managed_file`/
  `check_managed_region` no longer carry a duplicated five-line message-building block that could
  silently drift apart under a future one-sided edit; added a design-note paragraph to
  `check_managed_region`'s docstring explaining why the comparison target is always
  `recorded_sha` (the out-of-band state value) and never `span.sha` (the region's own
  attacker/editor-controlled begin-marker field) -- a hand-edit that changes the body can trivially
  hand-edit the adjacent marker's `sha=` to match, so comparing against it would defeat the
  modification check entirely (P-07; S-8.2's own `RegionSpan.sha` docstring already names this as
  a detect-stage guard's job, never the parser's). No behavior change from either edit.
  `src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/meta/test_p07_no_hash_comparison_in_apply.py` -- fixed
  a real detector bug both reviewers independently confirmed: `_hash_import_violations`'s
  `ast.ImportFrom` branch used a bare `module.endswith("detect.hashes")` with no package-boundary
  check, so an unrelated module merely ENDING in those characters (e.g.
  `mypkg.underdetect.hashes`, an unrelated `underdetect` package's own `hashes` submodule) would
  false-positive as a P-07 violation. Added `_ends_with_dotted_segment(dotted, segment)` (exact
  match, or `.`-prefixed suffix match) and routed all three violation checks through it uniformly;
  added a regression test proving the previously-vulnerable shape no longer fires.
- (note) Two other review findings were considered and rejected, not patched: (1) validating
  `recorded_sha`'s shape (8 lowercase hex) inside this module, so a malformed value could route to
  `FindingType.STATE_INVALID` instead of a content-mismatch finding -- rejected as out of this
  story's scope: `state/store.py` does not exist yet (a later epic), FR-104's own "validated
  against a JSON schema on every read" is that future story's job, not this pure comparison
  layer's, and every real sha producer in this package (`hash_content`, `region_sha`) already only
  ever emits the valid shape, so a malformed value can only originate from state corruption this
  module has no way to distinguish from a real hand-edit without reading state itself. (2) two
  edge-case findings about `region_body_text` given a `text`/`span` pair that do not correspond
  (span parsed from different text) -- rejected as caller-precondition misuse with no real call
  site yet (this story's own Never bullet: no consumer exists before S-9.6), consistent with this
  package's established "trust an already-parsed input" convention across `detect/`.
- (note) Re-verified after the patch: `pyforge-marshal-test` 3784 passed (9 slow deselected, +1 for
  the new regression test). `ruff check` unchanged at 187 errors package-wide (same pre-existing
  `I001` category, zero new findings from either edit). `pyright` on both changed files: zero errors
  in `hashes.py`; the meta-test file's one `reportMissingImports` on `import pyforge.marshal` is the
  same pre-existing category every sibling meta-test file already carries when checked in isolation
  (confirmed present identically in `test_ad19_no_adapter_branch.py`, unaffected by this pass's
  edit). `lint-imports` clean (3 contracts kept, 0 broken). `python scripts/spec_surface_reconcile.py`
  reconciled by this entry. Baseline re-stamped, scoped to this spec only.

- (note) ROOT CAUSE FOUND for "stuck orchestrator baseline" (2026-08-14, diagnosed against 9.6's own
  journal.jsonl in run `20260813-094919-bfcb`): `task.baseline_commit` is meant to stay fixed for a
  story's whole dev-retry loop (`engine.py` line ~1229's own comment says so), captured once via

  likely a bulk/periodic re-sync tied to sibling stories landing concurrently on the same shared
  target branch while 9.6 sat isolated. `_verify_shared_gates` (verify.py:1211-1235) then compares

  re-stamp). This is a `bmad_loop` package defect (pixi-installed, git-pinned, not owned by this
  repo) — out of scope to patch live against two actively-running loops; flagged for a Dream+Spec

- (change) Story 10.1 ("Copier engine wrapper — the single seam") landed by hand 2026-08-14: adds
  `seed/engine/copier.py`, the ONLY module in the package that imports `copier` (P-02) — exposes
  `materialize(MaterializeRequest) -> MaterializeResult`, which always renders into an ephemeral
  staging location (never the caller's live `dst_path`), then validates the staged output against
  an allow-list (the manifest's `copied-managed`/`copied-seeded`/`generated-derived`/
  `hybrid-managed-region` entries, union the fixed Genesis-owned set) before returning it as data —
  nothing is committed by this module. Staging is verb-dependent: `COPY` uses a fresh empty tmp
  dir; `UPDATE`/`RECOPY` use a local `git clone` of `dst_path` (cheap via hardlinks) since Copier's
  own diff-and-merge algorithm needs real commit history. Full real-Copier testing (`copier==9.17.1`
  against hand-built fixture templates, no mocking) plus an adversarial review pass (Blind Hunter +
  Edge Case Hunter). One **intent_gap** surfaced during triage: the Boundaries bullet "any path
  also matching the manifest's `never_write` globs is rejected even if otherwise allow-listed"
  directly contradicted two already-shipped `copied-seeded` manifest entries (`starter-dream` at
  `docs/dreams/{{ slug }}.md`, `specs-readme` at
  `_bmad-output/projects/{{ slug }}/planning-artifacts/specs/README.md`), making FR-74's
  Dream-seeding requirement permanently unsatisfiable as literally written. Per the intent_gap
  protocol the implementation was correctly reverted with nothing committed — but unlike a
  deferred-story timeout, this revert path preserves no git-recoverable artifact (no
  `attempt-preserve/*` branch, no `failed/*/changes.patch`). The `<intent-contract>` was amended
  per the dev session's own recommended fix (a manifest-declared path is now exempt from the
  `never_write` veto — `never_write` protects already-materialized content from being clobbered on
  a *later* run, not the manifest's own one-time explicit seeding), and the reviewed implementation
  was recovered by reconstructing it from the dev session's own Claude Code transcript rather than
  re-implementing from scratch: the adversarial-review Agent-tool prompts embedded the complete
  tracked-file diff plus full content of every new file (both reviewers received the identical
  diff, so either prompt sufficed as the recovery source), byte-identical to what the session
  actually tested. Files:
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/pixi.toml,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/pyproject.toml,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/engine/__init__.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/engine/copier.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/meta/test_p02_copier_sole_ownership.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_seed_engine_copier.py.
  `pyforge-marshal-test`: 4181 passed, 9 deselected. The missing-preservation gap this recovery
  depended on transcript archaeology to close is tracked as its own Dream:
  `docs/dreams/bmad-loop-intent-gap-work-preservation.md`.
- (event) Spec-surface reconciliation for the S-10.1 recovery landing complete as of this entry;
  baseline re-stamped `--spec pyforge-marshal/spec-pyforge-marshal` immediately after.
- (change) Story 10.3 ("The apply runner — transactional, guarded") implemented 2026-08-14 on
  `bmad-loop/20260814-202331-bc8d/10-3-the-apply-runner-transactional-guarded`, baseline
  `64e717d135`. `seed/apply/` was an empty package: Story 9.6 built a `Plan` carrying a
  `RepoFingerprint` explicitly so "a later `apply` story can refuse a `Plan` whose fingerprint no
  longer matches", and nothing consumed it, leaving AR-5 (stale plan applied to a changed repo) and
  NFR-R1 (no partial state) both unmitigated. `run_apply(plan, *, repo_root, never_write, commit)`
  is the transactional envelope and nothing else: it refuses a stale plan before touching disk
  (`PreconditionFailure`, exit 3, message carrying the literal `stale-plan` token and naming every
  divergence — the taxonomy's first real raise site for that leaf), executes `plan.actions` in the
  plan's own order through a caller-supplied per-action `commit`, snapshots `repo_root /
  action.target_path` immediately before each action, and on ANY `BaseException` (`KeyboardInterrupt`
  included) restores every snapshot in REVERSE order through `fs.write`/`fs.remove` before
  re-raising the original unchanged; a rollback that itself fails is reported loudly as
  `InternalError` (exit 10) naming the unrestored paths, chained from the original, because a failed
  rollback IS the partial state NFR-R1 forbids. **Two design tensions were resolved explicitly
  rather than silently.** (1) AD-57 requires apply to refuse on a fingerprint mismatch, and the
  fingerprint includes artifact hashes — but P-07 ("hash guards are checked in detect, never in
  apply") is enforced structurally by `tests/meta/test_p07_no_hash_comparison_in_apply.py`, an
  import ban on `seed/apply/**`. Resolution: the comparison lives in `plan/build.py` as
  `fingerprint_drift(plan, repo_root) -> tuple[str, ...]`, beside `build_plan`, the fingerprint's
  SOLE producer, so producer and verifier cannot drift apart; the meta test is untouched and
  genuinely green (no hashing under `seed/apply/`), and `run.py` imports the verdict, never the
  mechanism. The distinction is real: P-07 constrains the per-artifact hand-edit guard whose answer
  decides an artifact's fate, AD-57 asks whether the whole `Plan` is still true, with only
  proceed-all or refuse-all as outcomes. (2) "Empty plan is a no-op that exits 0" collides with
  "refuses a stale plan" when both hold; refusing wins, since an empty plan asserts "this repo needs
  nothing" and a moved repo is exactly what makes that assertion no longer known. `fingerprint_drift`
  reads each target UNCONDITIONALLY rather than through `_current_text`'s `ABSENT` gate — reusing
  that gate would have made the most dangerous case invisible (an artifact absent at plan time but
  created since would re-hash to `''`, match, and be silently clobbered); the shared
  `_read_text_or_blank` helper keeps the build-time and verify-time degradation rules one spelling.
  A review pass over the implementation closed one further hole: the `actions` ↔ `artifact_hashes`
  correspondence is now checked in BOTH directions, since `Plan.from_json_dict` validates action
  uniqueness/order but never cross-checks the fingerprint, so a hand-edited `plan.json` that DROPPED
  a pair would have left that artifact silently unverified. Deliberately NOT built here, each with a
  named owner: staged-output reconciliation and staging-directory cleanup, despite
  `engine/copier.py`'s docstring assigning both to "Story 10.3's apply runner" —
  `MaterializeResult` exposes only absolute `staged_paths`, never the staging root, so neither is
  implementable against the shipped API (routed to whoever wires materialization, S-10.6); all
  preconditions other than the fingerprint (S-10.4, whose own `Surface:` line names this same file);
  and removal of empty parent directories `atomic_write_bytes` created (`fs` ships no
  directory-removal primitive and P-01 forbids reaching around it — rollback restores file CONTENT,
  so an empty directory may survive). Story 10.2's `seed/state/` is NOT in this baseline (it landed
  `done` but deferred on an unrelated `pyforge-mason` gate), so `run.py` imports nothing from
  `seed.state` and a test asserts it, keeping "state is untouched" structurally true through the
  eventual merge. Files:
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/apply/__init__.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/apply/run.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/plan/build.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_seed_apply_run.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_seed_plan_build.py.
  The adversarial review pass (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter, no shared context, both verifying
  by executing the real code) returned 0 intent_gap, 0 bad_spec, 11 patches, 7 defers, 1 reject —
  and caught TWO high-severity holes worth recording. First: `fingerprint_drift` compared content
  only, and since every read degrades an absent/non-file/unreadable/non-UTF-8 target to `''` — the
  same value an `ABSENT` artifact's recorded hash already encodes — a binary, unreadable, or empty
  file appearing at an absent artifact's path re-hashed identically, the plan was accepted as
  fresh, and apply destroyed it. That is verbatim the hazard the function's own docstring claimed
  to have closed by dropping the state gate; the gate was never the whole problem, the degradation
  inside the read was. Fixed by comparing STATE where content cannot decide (an `ABSENT` artifact
  must still not exist; a present one must still be readable as text). Second: `run_apply`
  performed no containment check on `action.target_path`, and `Path(repo_root) / "/etc/passwd"`
  discards `repo_root` entirely — so a hand-edited `plan.json` (explicitly untrusted input) could
  direct snapshots and rollback writes anywhere on the host, with `fs`'s never-write guard unable
  to catch it because a path outside `repo_root` falls back to an absolute POSIX string no
  repo-relative pattern matches. Now an `escaping-target` refusal before any write, mirroring
  `detect/inventory.py::_resolve_within_repo`. The pass also corrected two docstrings that
  overclaimed: the module no longer says its design makes P-04 "literally true", but states
  plainly that P-04 and P-07 are deliberately REINTERPRETED here (apply forms no per-artifact
  opinion; only proceed-all or refuse-all), so a green P-07 meta test is not misread as proof the
  rule was untouched. Seven bounds this story does not close are filed as `DW-FU-10-3` …
  `DW-FU-10-3-7`, the first of which independently corroborates `DW-FU-9-6-2`.
  `pyforge-marshal-test`: 4237 passed, 9 deselected (baseline 4181). `git diff --stat --
  tests/meta/` empty — no meta test edited, and the P-07 guard now parametrizes over `run.py` and
  passes unmodified.

- (change) 2026-08-14 — story `10-3-the-apply-runner-transactional-guarded` VERIFICATION-REPAIR
  pass. One file changed outside the story's declared Surface:
  `tests/packaging/test_dependency_completeness.py` — added
  `CONDA_ONLY_RUN_DEPS["pyforge-mason"] = {"conda-lock", "gh", "pixi", "python-build", "twine"}`.
  No `seed/` source, no meta test, no `pyproject.toml`, and no `pixi.toml` was touched; the
  story's `<intent-contract>` is unmodified. Why an out-of-surface edit was the right call:
  `DW-FU-10-2` had parked `test_conda_run_deps_add_nothing_undeclared[pyforge-mason]` as a
  pre-existing red belonging to "whoever owns Mason's packaging gate", and Story 10.2 deferred on
  the same reasoning one story earlier. But `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-ci pyforge-deps-test` is
  one of the loop's DETERMINISTIC verify commands, so the deferral was not parking debt — it was
  refusing to let 10.3 (and every story behind it) land at all. "Pre-existing" answers *did I break
  this*; it never answers *should this stay*. The remedy is the one the ledger entry itself
  predicted and the assertion message names: justify, don't declare. Verified rather than assumed —
  all five engines are located with `shutil.which` and driven by `subprocess.run` from
  `engines/*.py`, and a repo-wide import grep over `pyforge-mason/src/` returns nothing for any of
  them, so mason never IMPORTS them. (Precise claim, corrected by the second review pass: only
  `pixi` and `gh` are non-Python binaries; `twine`, `conda-lock`, and `python-build` ARE importable
  PyPI distributions mason merely drives as subprocesses — a property of the code, so the second
  pass made it an assertion rather than a trusted comment.) The alternative branch
  (declaring them in `[project.dependencies]`) would put five CLI tools on every
  `pip install pyforge-mason`, against FR-41's lean-dependency floor. This is the sanctioned escape
  hatch on established precedent — the identical shape is already recorded there for
  `pyforge-warden`'s `deptry`/`osv-scanner` and `pyforge-steward`'s `age`, the latter landed by
  marshal's OWN Story 4.11, so a marshal story editing this table for a sibling package is not new.
  The gate is not weakened: `CONDA_ONLY_RUN_DEPS` feeds only `_conda_only`, and the five version
  ranges stay pinned to `engines/__init__.py`'s `SpecifierSet` constants by
  `pyforge-mason/tests/meta/test_engine_version_range_sync.py` (all five pairs green).
  `DW-FU-10-2` moved `open` -> `resolved`. Post-repair gates: `pyforge-deps-test` 74 passed (from
  1 failed / 73 passed), `pyforge-marshal-test` 4237 passed / 9 deselected, `pyforge-mason-test`
  1409 passed / 2 deselected, `git diff --stat -- src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/meta/`
  empty, `spec_surface_reconcile.py` unchanged at its 2 pre-existing
  `pyforge-mason/spec-django-accelerator-framework` findings (human `--write-baseline` remedy,
  genuinely not closable unattended).

- (change) 2026-08-14 — story `10-3-the-apply-runner-transactional-guarded` SECOND REVIEW PASS
  (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter, no shared context, both verifying by execution). Changed:
  `src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/apply/run.py`,
  `.../seed/plan/build.py`, `.../tests/unit/test_seed_apply_run.py`,
  `.../tests/unit/test_seed_plan_build.py`, `tests/packaging/test_dependency_completeness.py`.
  0 intent_gap, 0 bad_spec, 11 patches, 3 defers, 4 rejects. TWO high-severity holes, both in code
  the FIRST review pass had added, and both worth recording because they share a shape: a guard
  that does not bind what it guards.
  (1) The containment check resolved `repo_root / action.target_path`, but the snapshot loop and
  `_restore` both re-derived the RAW path — so the check constrained a value nothing downstream
  used. Both reviewers found it independently and both destroyed a real file proving it: with
  `sub/../b.txt` the path resolved inside the repo, stat'd absent (no `sub`), snapshotted `None`,
  and after `commit`'s `mkdir(parents=True)` the same raw path resolved onto the real `b.txt`,
  which rollback deleted — an unrelated pre-existing in-repo file destroyed by a rolled-back run,
  exactly NFR-R1's prohibition; and a `commit` materializing a missing parent as a symlink to a
  host directory made rollback `fs.remove` a file entirely outside `repo_root`, which `fs`'s
  never-write guard structurally cannot catch (an out-of-repo path degrades to an absolute POSIX
  string no repo-relative pattern matches — the very reason the check existed). Fixed on both
  sides: non-normalized targets refused before resolution, and `_restore` re-checks containment
  per path, reporting an escaped target as unrestorable rather than touching it.
  (2) `fingerprint_drift`'s ABSENT branch had gained `or target.is_symlink()`, making the VERIFIER
  stricter than the PRODUCER — and a dangling symlink is where they disagree, because `exists()`
  follows the broken link (so `build_plan` records ABSENT) while `is_symlink()` does not. The plan
  was refused the instant it was produced, re-planning reproduced it, and apply was unreachable
  with a remedy string that could not work. Predicate restored to `exists()` alone. The general
  lesson both share: producer/verifier agreement is the whole reason `fingerprint_drift` was put
  beside `build_plan`, and a guard added in review is not exempt from being checked against the
  code path it is supposed to constrain.
  Also fixed: NUL-in-path raised a raw `ValueError` past the six-leaf taxonomy; a target resolving
  to the repo ROOT was accepted as success; the escaping-target remedy asserted such plans "did not
  come from `build_plan`" when `build_plan` demonstrably emits them (`_resolve_within_repo` → None
  → ABSENT, raw path retained); duplicate ids in `artifact_hashes` went unreported while the
  actions-side mirror was refused; and the packaging repair's own `CONDA_ONLY_RUN_DEPS` entry was
  unratcheted — now guarded by `test_conda_only_entries_are_still_conda_run_deps` plus
  `test_conda_only_run_deps_are_never_imported`, which checks the `deferred` import bucket both
  pre-existing call sites discard, closing a path where a lazy `import twine` would ship an
  unusable wheel silently. Both mutation-verified. 9 regression tests added; 8 of them verified to
  FAIL against the pre-fix code (the 9th replaces a monkeypatched `ProcessError` assertion with a
  real-path one, so it passes both ways by design). Three bounds filed as `DW-FU-10-3-8` …
  `DW-FU-10-3-10`. Gates: `pyforge-deps-test` 84 passed, `pyforge-marshal-test` 4246 passed / 9
  deselected (from 4237), `pyforge-mason-test` 1409 passed / 2 deselected,
  `git diff --stat -- src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/meta/` empty,
  `spec_surface_reconcile.py` unchanged at its 2 pre-existing findings.
- (change) Story 10.4 ("Preconditions, refusals, and skips") landed 2026-08-14: gives
  `errors.PreconditionFailure` (exit 3) its first raise sites since Story 7.2, and gives `--skip`
  its first existence outside a remedy string. Adds `seed/verbs/preconditions.py` — a caller-side
  refusal ladder, `check_preconditions(...)`, that a mutating verb calls BEFORE `run_apply`, with
  six rungs in a fixed order: (1) not a git repo, (2) dirty worktree, (3) target escapes
  `repo_root`, (4) target matches `never_write`, (5) target is a symlink, (6) managed content
  hand-edited. `dry_run` bypasses rung 2 ONLY; `force` bypasses rung 6 ONLY; rung 4 is bypassable
  by neither flag. Rung 6 iterates the caller's `ManagedRecord`s rather than the plan's actions —
  `classify` marks a present `copied-managed` artifact PRESENT_CONFORMANT whatever its bytes say
  (P-07), so a hand-edited managed FILE never appears in the plan at all and a plan-driven check
  would silently pass exactly the case SC-04 exists to catch — and reports ALL divergences in one
  message. Adds `seed/verbs/skips.py` — `first_match` (the verbs layer's single `fnmatch.fnmatchcase`
  glob semantic), `record_skip` (state-side, returns the updated tuple its caller persists) and
  `apply_skips` (plan-side, pure). Extends `seed/plan/types.py` additively with frozen
  `SkippedArtifact` + `Plan.skipped`, so a skip lands in the serialized artifact a human reviews as
  a diff rather than only in a rendering; `apply_skips` drops the skipped artifact's pair from
  `RepoFingerprint.artifact_hashes` as it moves the action, keeping the plan consistent for
  Story 10.3's bidirectional `fingerprint_drift`. The ladder lives in `seed/verbs/`, NOT
  `seed/apply/`, for two independent reasons: `tests/meta/test_p07_no_hash_comparison_in_apply.py`
  bans `hashlib` and every `detect.hashes` import spelling under `seed/apply/**` with no allow-list
  (rung 6 exists to call `detect.hashes`), and `seed/apply/run.py` does not exist on this baseline —
  it is created by the unmerged sibling Story 10.3, so authoring it here would be an add/add merge
  collision. Two duplicated semantics (`plan.build._repo_is_dirty`, `fs._matches`) are pinned by
  agreement tests rather than hoisted into a shared home, since both live in files this story may
  not edit. `tests/packaging/test_dependency_completeness.py` was adopted BYTE-IDENTICAL from the
  sibling 10.3 branch (`git checkout <branch> -- <path>`) rather than re-authored, closing
  `DW-FU-10-2` while making the eventual merge a clean no-op instead of a conflict. A parallel
  adversarial review (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter, no shared context) found six real defects,
  all fixed and regression-tested: rungs 3-5 evaluated per-ACTION rather than per-RUNG, so the
  reported rung depended on artifact-id sort order instead of the ladder (now three separate passes);
  a padded skip glob silently matched nothing (now stripped, matching `NeverWrite`'s own
  construction-time strip); a bare `str` passed as `Sequence[str]` iterated to characters and
  silently skipped EVERY action (now `UsageError`); an embedded-NUL `target_path` escaped as an
  untyped `ValueError` from `Path.resolve()` instead of a typed refusal; a managed file replaced by
  a dangling symlink passed rung 6 as "absent" (an SC-04 silent pass, since `Path.exists()` follows
  links); and the multi-divergence message counted divergences as artifacts. Rejected as
  out-of-scope with reasons recorded: `RepoFingerprint` integrity validation (Story 9.6/10.3
  surface), a seventh directory-target rung (the six are frozen by the spec), and parent-chain
  symlink resolution (inherited deliberately from `fs._guard`; narrowing it means narrowing
  `fs.py`, that module's story). Files:
  tests/packaging/test_dependency_completeness.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/plan/types.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/verbs/preconditions.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/verbs/skips.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_seed_plan_types.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_seed_verbs_preconditions.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_seed_verbs_skips.py.
  `pyforge-marshal-test`: 4403 passed, 9 deselected (baseline 4181 passed, 9 deselected).
  `pyforge-deps-test`: 84 passed (baseline `1 failed, 73 passed` on
  `test_conda_run_deps_add_nothing_undeclared[pyforge-mason]`, red at `64e717d`).
  `tests/meta/`, `seed/apply/`, `seed/plan/build.py` and `tests/unit/test_seed_plan_build.py` are
  all byte-unchanged against `64e717d`, the merge-safety condition against the unmerged sibling.

- (change) Story 10.4 second review pass (2026-08-14, no intent-contract change): a second round of
  parallel adversarial review found fifteen further defects, all fixed and regression-tested.
  Rung 6's two silent passes: `_read_managed_text` reported "cannot verify" as "absent" whenever
  `Path.is_symlink()`/`Path.exists()` swallowed an `OSError` (an unreadable PARENT directory,
  `ELOOP`), so a hand-edit under a locked directory passed — now a single `lstat()` inside
  `try/except` separates `FileNotFoundError` (absent, passes) from every other `OSError`
  (divergence); and `_region_divergences` iterated only `record.region_shas`, so a valid managed
  region hand-INSERTED into a hybrid file with no entry in state was reported as conformant — now
  every parsed-but-unrecorded region name is a divergence too. The skip↔rung-6 seam got its missing
  affordance, `skips.managed_after_skips(managed, plan)`: rungs 3-5 are skip-aware for free (they
  walk `plan.actions`), rung 6 is not (it walks the caller's records, by contract), so `--skip`
  could not protect a hand-edit and the refusal offered only `--force`, which discards every
  hand-edit including that one. `check_preconditions` deliberately still checks EVERY record it is
  handed; the filtering is the caller's. `skips.py` also: validation hoisted above the loop (it only
  ran when there were actions, so the bare-`str` typo passed cleanly on an empty plan), `patterns`
  materialized exactly once (a generator was consumed twice, silently dropping every existing
  pattern), a whitespace-only pattern now `UsageError` rather than an `""` that matches nothing, a
  lexical POSIX normalization before matching (`./AGENTS.md` was not skipped by the very pattern
  rung 4's refusal printed), and `dataclasses.replace` in place of the two field-by-field rebuilds.
  `preconditions.py` also: a misspelled `repo_root` was refused with "install git" (subprocess maps
  a missing `cwd` to `FileNotFoundError`) — now checked first, inside rung 1, mirroring `fs._guard`;
  a target normalizing to `.`/`""` cleared all three structural rungs and reached the runner; the
  never-write remedy's "not overridable, by --force or otherwise" overclaimed against the
  symlinked-ancestor bound `fs.py` documents; and the module docstring's "force says nothing about
  containment" was inverted — under `--force` NO rung validates a managed record's path at all.
  `plan/types.py` also: `repo_fingerprint`'s VALUE is now parsed in emission order (a malformed
  fingerprint was masked by a later `skipped` error, making the file's own ordering comment false),
  and `SkippedArtifact` rejects blank fields, matching `NeverWrite`/`record_skip`. The duplicated
  `_GIT_TIMEOUT_S` is now pinned by the same agreement test that pins `_is_dirty`. Files:
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/plan/types.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/verbs/preconditions.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/verbs/skips.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_seed_plan_types.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_seed_verbs_preconditions.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_seed_verbs_skips.py.
  `pyforge-marshal-test`: 4456 passed, 9 deselected (was 4403 passed). `pyforge-deps-test`: 84
  passed, with `tests/packaging/test_dependency_completeness.py` still byte-identical to the sibling
  10.3 branch. `tests/meta/`, `seed/apply/`, `seed/plan/build.py`, `tests/unit/test_seed_plan_build.py`
  and `seed/fs.py` are all byte-unchanged against `64e717d` — the merge-safety condition holds, and
  `fs.py`'s symlink-resolution semantics stay filed as `DW-FU-10-4` rather than fixed here.

  since the Spec landed and the stamp records exactly the state it was authored against.
- (change) Story 10.4, second review pass (2026-08-15) — one HIGH refusal-ladder hole closed in
  `seed/verbs/preconditions.py`. Rung 5 asked `Path.is_symlink()`, which swallows every `OSError`
  and answers `False`; `False` is that rung's one PASSING answer, so under an unreadable parent
  (`EACCES`) or a symlink loop (`ELOOP`) a real symlink cleared the rung and the runner would write
  THROUGH it — to a destination that can sit outside the repo, which defeats SC-05 because git
  cannot undo a write it never saw. The identical hazard had already been closed one function away
  in `_read_managed_text` for rung 6 during the FIRST review pass, and that function's docstring
  documents it; rung 5 was simply never given the same treatment, so the two rungs disagreed about
  what "cannot tell" means. Rung 5 now uses `lstat()` inside a `try`: `FileNotFoundError` is absence
  (passes — the ordinary create case), any other `OSError` refuses fail-closed. Mechanism was
  confirmed by execution BEFORE the fix (`is_symlink()` → `False` for a real symlink under a `0o000`
  parent, where `lstat()` raises `PermissionError`). Also added `_link_target`, because `lstat` and
  `readlink` are not atomic and a vanished link made a raw `OSError` escape the ladder in place of a
  `PreconditionFailure` (exit 3, with a remedy); the destination now degrades to `<unreadable>`
  instead of the refusal degrading to a crash. The module docstring's stale "rung 5's
  `is_symlink()`" reference was corrected to `lstat()`. Files:
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/src/pyforge/marshal/seed/verbs/preconditions.py,
  src/shared/packages/pyforge-marshal/tests/unit/test_seed_verbs_preconditions.py.
  `pyforge-marshal-test`: 4459 passed, 9 deselected (was 4456; +3 pinning tests).
  `pyforge-deps-test`: 84 passed. All four merge-safety diffs against `64e717d` remain empty.
  Three findings deferred with evidence rather than patched (`DW-FU-10-4-3`, `DW-FU-10-4-4`,
  `DW-FU-10-4-5`): each needs either a change to the FROZEN six-rung contract or a design decision
  owned by the verb-wiring story, and none has a production caller today.
- (event) The legacy-`plan.json` finding was RE-VERIFIED rather than inherited as rejected. The
  first pass rejected it on the premise "no `plan.json` is written by any production path today",
  and Stories 10.2 and 10.3 have since landed on `main` — which could have falsified it. Checked
  against `main`: `write_plan`/`load_plan` are still library-only, with no caller anywhere in
  `src/`, so the premise and the rejection both still hold. Recorded because a rejection built on a
  fact that later changed is invisible unless someone re-tests the fact.

- (note by claude) Story 30.5, 2026-09-06 (spec-bmad-611-era-alignment CAP-12 / spec-bmad-suite-lifecycle CAP-10): mid-implementation, the story's own central premise -- rename `harness_bmadloop.py`'s vendored `[dev] skill = "bmad-dev-auto"` literal to `"bmad-build-auto"` -- was found FALSE against the installed `bmad_loop` 0.11.1 package's own contract: `bmad_loop/policy.py`'s `DevPolicy.skill` is a permanent internal adapter discriminator (`DEV_SKILLS = {"bmad-dev-auto"}`, hard-validated by `PolicyError`), NOT a "which skill name is current" field -- the actually-invoked skill is resolved separately at runtime (`Engine._dev_skill()` / `install.dev_primitive_or_default`), and upstream's own comment states "a project on either era works with this field untouched." Attempting the rename threw `PolicyError` in `test_harness_policy_render.py` and would have broken every real loop-home's `bmad-loop validate`. Independently re-verified by the orchestrator by reading the installed package source directly (not just trusting the implementation subagent's report). Consequently `harness_bmadloop.py`, `test_harness_policy_render.py`, and `test_no_retired_bmad_skill_ids.py`'s SCAN_GLOBS widening were all reverted/never applied -- this means steward's CAP-9 `_shim_retirement_blockers` (Story 14.9) has a latent false-positive bug: its naive text-match against this exact permanent literal will refuse `--no-shims` forever, since the literal can never legitimately change. This story instead landed only the parts of its own scope that were independently true regardless of the false premise: two dead file-path citations (`chain.py`, `marshal-policy.toml`, both citing skill directories that moved under the 6.12 rename) and two present-tense docstring/prose renames (`cli/spin.py` + its `test_spin.py` mirror, `docs/dreams/README.md`) — all comment/docstring/doc-only, no behavior change, `pyforge-marshal-test` 7510 passed. The CAP-9 detector bug and the still-unresolved `--no-shims` readiness gap are reported to the operator as a blocker to Session 2 step 9, not fixed here (fixing CAP-9 lives in `pyforge-steward`, explicitly out of this spec's own surface and this story's own Never-boundary). No scoped re-stamp needed for `harness_bmadloop.py`/`test_harness_policy_render.py`/`test_no_retired_bmad_skill_ids.py` since none of them changed; `cli/spin.py`/`test_spin.py` reconciled below.

Docstring-only, no assertion or behavior change. Reconciled + baseline re-stamped under this spec's surface per the foreign-spec-surface procedure.

- (event by claude) status: (absent) -> shipped (fleet-readiness-decision-batch-2026-09-09 Class B, row mars-B-B13, operator-approved). This is the station canonical Spec and it has carried NO `status:` line at all -- one of the eight fleet-wide status-less Specs, three of which are marshal. 195 stories across 32 epics are decomposed from it and the CLI it contracts is the estate live engine, so `shipped` is the honest value. The `id:`-form frontmatter is kept; a real `open_questions:` key is added by the disposition pass below, because until today every frontmatter-reading detector saw ZERO open questions on a Spec whose own body says its architecture gate returned BLOCKED-ON.
- (decision by claude) 2026-09-09 DISPOSITION PASS OPENED over the § Open Questions BLOCKED-ON block (adversarial review 2026-07-25, 6 CRITICAL / 12 HIGH / 11 MED / 3 LOW), item by item, against live code at fe4025ea90 (fleet-readiness-decision-batch-2026-09-09 row mars-B-B13 / D-1). The block has been carried in the BODY since 2026-08-02 and never dispositioned. Ruling: each of F-1..F-6 and Q-11..Q-16 gets a live/resolved/moot verdict with evidence (the twelve entries that follow), and the STILL-LIVE ones are HOISTED into a real `open_questions:` frontmatter key on the bmad-spec re-derive so detectors can see them. Rejected: mark the whole block historical without per-item disposition -- F-4 alone (an undeclared trust model behind operator-attributed journal entries) is still a live property of shipped code.
- (decision by claude) F-1 (composed policy has no path to the harness) -- RESOLVED. `marshal config --write-harness-policy` shipped (cli/config.py:327) as Story 1.10 (ledger 1-10-render-the-harness-policy-from-the-canonical-effectivepolicy: done); the rendering path and its idempotence are contracted at adapters/harness_bmadloop.py:517,:568 and core/harness_profile.py:830, and cli/spin.py:706 reads the last persisted render to detect divergence. The conveyance has a story and a boundary. Auto-memory project_loop_home_policy_render records the operator-facing form (`marshal config --write-harness-policy .` per loop home after PR #139). RETIRE from the block.
- (decision by claude) F-2 (a quarantine clause legislates a false green into the run-truth journal) -- RESOLVED, replaced by SCOPED unevaluability exactly as the finding demanded. core/journal.py quarantines to the offending `(story, kind)` DOMAIN rather than the run (:586), surfaces it as a registered MRS-JOURNAL-001/002 Finding under AD-8 ("unevaluable is a failure, never silently dropped", :626), widens to the whole run only when a partial recovery carries no meaningful scope (AD-30, :620-624), and the two live review findings that drove it are recorded in the code itself (:586 the sidecar round-trip collision, :925 the blank terminator that made an intact run wholly unevaluable). RETIRE from the block.
- (decision by claude) F-3 (standalone gate evaluation has no frozen set) -- RESOLVED. cli/gate.py is the standalone verify-command runner: `--run <id>` folds that run real journal (:41), and a standalone invocation without one declares its own scope explicitly -- data["scope"] = "policy-seed-only" plus data["scope_note"] = "mid-run freezes not visible" (:826-831), with the code citing AD-26/F-3 by name and AD-26 own resolution text ("a complete, legitimate answer on its own, not a degraded one"). The "evaluate before approving with no run in flight" journey now exits 0 against a declared, narrower scope. RETIRE from the block.
- (decision by claude) F-4 (the trust model is undeclared) -- STILL LIVE, and the sharpest of the six. The escape hatch for privileged changes is an operator-attributed journal entry, and core/journal.py:159 is the ONLY place in the package that names the concept ("policy- or operator-attributed only (enforced at the CALL surface)"). No authentication primitive is defined anywhere in pyforge-marshal, the worktree is explicitly not a sandbox, and process isolation is deferred -- so a governed session can invoke the CLI or write the record directly. The Spec must either STATE that the agent is trusted (and that the guarantee is therefore advisory) or SPECIFY what makes attribution unforgeable. HOIST into open_questions:.
- (decision by claude) F-5 (mid-run freeze accumulation has no writer in either production gate mode) -- PARTIALLY RESOLVED; the residue is LIVE. The vocabulary and the writer constraint shipped: KIND_FREEZE_DECLARED / KIND_FREEZE_REMOVED are journal entry kinds (core/journal.py:152-163) whose payloads name the declaring story key (:655-656), and the call surface admits policy- or operator-attributed writes only (:159). What the finding actually asked is still unanswered: under gate modes `none` and `per-epic` NO OPERATOR IS PRESENT and the story may not declare its own freeze, so the only live writer is the policy seed -- and cli/gate.py:831 records that a standalone evaluation cannot even see a mid-run freeze. HOIST the narrowed question: name the unattended writer, or state that mid-run freezes require per-story approval and amend the affected requirements.
- (decision by claude) F-6 (a unique monotonic entry id is unachievable under the declared append protocol) -- RESOLVED exactly as the finding proposed. core/journal.py:13 documents a COMPOSITE ``(writer_id, counter)`` id, "a per-writer monotonic counter, never across the run" (:201), serialized at :229-233, parsed at :1024, with the sidecar path derived from it at :421-433. The pairing and total-order rules now rest on a composite id rather than an unachievable global monotonic one. RETIRE from the block.
- (decision by claude) Q-11 (should fleet-level resource budgets be v1 scope?) -- STILL LIVE, but NARROWED by this batch. Per-run and per-story ceilings ship (core/policy.py:503-505 max_tokens_per_run 500M, max_wall_clock_minutes_per_run 2880, plus the per-story pair; core/dispatch.py:257-259 reads them); no cross-run or fleet budget exists anywhere. Two 2026-09-09 rulings bound it without answering it: the enforceable dispatch budget signal is wall-clock plus idle-strand with token ceilings advisory (spec-marshal-single-story-dispatch OQ-3), and fan-out concurrency gets its own `dispatch.max_parallel` key (spec-marshal-parallel-dispatch-fanout CAP-6, Story 33.8) rather than a token-budget-aware cap. HOIST, restated: is a cross-run budget in scope at all, given it would be the first thing to need shared mutable state and therefore the first to break the loop-home write boundary?
- (decision by claude) Q-12 (is OpenTelemetry gen_ai.* emission worth v1 cost?) -- STILL LIVE and UNSTARTED. Verified 2026-09-09: zero occurrences of `gen_ai` or `opentelemetry` anywhere in the pyforge-marshal package. The premise that deferred it (the conventions remain Development-stability with live attribute renames) is unchanged. HOIST unchanged.
- (decision by claude) Q-13 (what triggers migrating the adapter layer to ACP?) -- STILL LIVE; ONE OF THE THREE PROPOSED TRIGGERS HAS FIRED, which is itself new information. Proposed triggers were: the harness gains an ACP client path, OR two must-support adapters ship ACP-only, OR the schema reaches stable with the Claude adapter known gaps closed. bmad-loop 0.9.0 shipped a sanctioned `copilot` profile plus `copilot --acp` (recorded in docs/specs/copilot-bridge-vscode-extension.md supersession), so trigger one is live upstream -- while pyforge-marshal itself contains zero `acp` references. HOIST, restated: trigger one has fired upstream; does that alone start the migration, or does marshal wait for the schema to reach stable?
- (decision by claude) Q-14 (does the 25-minute idle threshold false-positive on legitimately slow verify steps?) -- STILL LIVE; still "needs one wave of data" and the wave has not been collected. The default is real and operator-tunable through the normal policy layers (core/policy.py:475 idle_threshold_minutes = 25, validator at :1375-1392, Story 3.5 / FR-12). No station overrides it. HOIST unchanged, with the cheapest closure named: the dispatch journals under _bmad-output/projects/<slug>/implementation-artifacts/dispatch-runs/ now carry per-session timing at source, so the wave is a read, not an experiment.
- (decision by claude) Q-15 (verb naming) -- RESOLVED for the dispatch family, 2026-09-09. `marshal factory dispatch` is the shipped verb and there is no `marshal dev` family; the branch grammar dispatch/<project_slug>/<key> is a shared-spine constant (pyforge-core landing_evidence.py:50) consumed by doctor, so renaming would break a cross-package grammar (fleet-readiness-decision-batch-2026-09-09 row mars-B-OQ1, closed on spec-marshal-single-story-dispatch). This Spec Q-15 inherits that answer. RETIRE from the block.
- (decision by claude) Q-16 (what minimal story exercises spec -> change -> verify -> commit as the conformance smoke?) -- RESOLVED. Story 6.5 "conformance smoke in an ephemeral home" is `done` (ledger 6-5-conformance-smoke-in-an-ephemeral-home: done), and the pinned smoke story `1-1-marshal-conformance-smoke` is the artifact the token-economy benchmark (Story 33.1) records its off-leg and on-leg against. Adapter-agnostic and cheap, exactly as the question required. RETIRE from the block.
- (decision by claude) 2026-09-09 DISPOSITION PASS CLOSED. RETIRED as resolved or moot: F-1, F-2, F-3, F-6, Q-15, Q-16. HOISTED into a real `open_questions:` frontmatter key on the re-derive: F-4 (undeclared trust model), F-5-narrowed (no unattended mid-run freeze writer), Q-11 (fleet-level budgets), Q-12 (OTel gen_ai.*), Q-13 (ACP trigger one has fired upstream), Q-14 (the 25-minute idle threshold needs its wave). Items 7 (the pre-Epic-1 amendment set), 8 (the epic-scoped HIGH findings), 9 (cross-tool entry-file family ownership) and the three folded genesis-installer questions (K-03, seed init creates a repo or a tree, append-at-EOF as a safe anchor fallback) are NOT dispositioned by this pass -- they are epic-scoped or installer-scoped and belong to their own epics; they stay in the body. This pass covers exactly the six BLOCKED-ON decisions and the six numbered station questions the batch named.
- (event) (self-validate pass 1 -- coherence) 2026-09-09 re-derive: PASS. status: (absent) -> shipped ADDED; a real open_questions: key now carries the SIX still-live items (F-4, F-5-narrowed, Q-11, Q-12, Q-13, Q-14) as folded block scalars, parsed clean by yaml.safe_load. The six retired items keep their original text struck through with a dated resolution rather than being deleted. 18 CAP ids, unchanged; 7 companions, 4 sources, surface unchanged.
- (event) (self-validate pass 2 -- preservation) 2026-09-09 re-derive: PASS with ONE FLAG FOR THE OPERATOR. All fifteen 2026-09-09 entries landed. Items 7, 8, 9 and the three folded genesis-installer questions (K-03; creates-a-repo-or-a-tree; append-at-EOF) are verified still present and untouched, as the closing entry directed. FLAG: the memlog dispositions Q-15 as THE VERB-NAMING question (which is how spec-marshal-single-story-dispatch OQ-1 and PRD Q-15 refer to it), but this Spec's BODY item 15 as written asks a different question -- where a story's declared difficulty lives. The verb-naming resolution is recorded and retired as instructed; the difficulty-location question is NOT answered by it, so it was left in the body unanswered and NOT hoisted, with the discrepancy flagged inline for an operator ruling on which question the number denotes. Nothing was silently conflated or dropped.
- (decision by claude) F-4 ANSWERED 2026-09-09 (operator, after the fleet-readiness re-derive): B is the CONTRACT — operator attribution on journal entries becomes unforgeable before the Track (hub:CAP-3, Story 33.4) serves a second principal; A is the v1 STATE — today the governed agent is trusted and attribution is advisory, enforced at the call surface only (core/journal.py:159), with process isolation deferred and named. Vessel: Story 33.11 (Epic 33), Deps S-33.4, minted blocked on the trigger (Foundry cutover flag, a shared Hub, or an external adopter) so it never jumps the throughput queue. Remove from open_questions; record the dated answer in the body.
- (decision by claude) F-5 (narrowed) ANSWERED 2026-09-09 (operator): under gate modes none and per-epic there is NO unattended mid-run freeze writer by design — a mid-run freeze requires per-story approval; the policy seed is the only unattended freeze source (cli/gate.py:831 stands). Amend the affected requirements to state it. Remove from open_questions.
- (decision by claude) Q-11 ANSWERED 2026-09-09 (operator): a cross-run (fleet-level) resource budget is OUT of scope for v1 — it would be the first shared mutable state across loop homes and break the loop-home write boundary; per-run/per-story ceilings (core/policy.py:503-505) plus dispatch.max_parallel (fanout CAP-6, Story 33.8) and the wall-clock+idle-strand dispatch signal are the v1 controls; revisit only after Story 33.1's measured baseline exists. Remove from open_questions.
- (decision by claude) Q-12 ANSWERED 2026-09-09 (operator): OpenTelemetry gen_ai.* emission is deferred until the semantic conventions reach stable; the Track publisher (Story 33.4) is v1's telemetry seam and maps to gen_ai.* later without re-instrumenting. Remove from open_questions.

- (capability by claude) CAP-244 — a landing never re-dispatches the story it just landed. Intent: the fleet campaign supervisor treats the session-exited-but-not-yet-promoted window as in-flight, and a self-refusal of the already-merged kind advances the campaign instead of blocking it. Source: docs/dreams/pyforge-marshal.md 2026-09-18 Realization-log entry (four manual relaunches on the herald Epic 23 drain, campaigns …151925342Z-82ce96c8 / …162241936Z-bfc1f666).
- (capability by claude) CAP-245 — a harness's own usage-wall wording is a transient outcome. Intent: `classify_session_log` recognises Cursor's live 'You're out of usage … increase your limit' text (and Claude Code's weekly/monthly limit text) as `quota_exceeded`, so the fleet planner classifies the block transient. Source: run pyforge-herald-20260918T132400673Z-194af3a0 (three-second cursor death classified terminal).
- (capability by claude) CAP-246 — `--harness` outranks a dead tier-map harness. Intent: an explicit invocation flag leads the harness walk over an inline-table tier-map harness without a policy edit, with the model resolved for the harness actually chosen (spec-cursor-native-tier-map CAP-1's fails-safe preserved). Source: herald PR #1458 was needed because the flag could not move the station.
- (capability by claude) CAP-247 — landing evidence carries the station in every shape. Intent: the AD-24 templated merge subject carries the station slug by default (`Merge {slug}/{key} into main`, herald's #1458 form) and the un-scoped `Story N.M:` shape needs branch/station corroboration; live history is grandfathered through the SHA/recovery allowlist. Source: atlas's `Merge 23-N into main` ×7 marked herald 23.x merged (doctor 23.1–23.3 one-second no-op dispatches, 2026-09-18 morning); steward's `Story 48.2:`/`48.4:` poison marshal 48.x today.
- (capability by claude) CAP-248 — the promoter reads a spec through its banner. Intent: `is_valid_spec_text` / `parse_declared_surface` accept a leading `<!-- … -->` block before frontmatter so `_already_promoted_keys` never mistakes a banner-topped tracked spec for unpromoted. Source: local commit b0b7f3019f (finalize re-promoted herald's stale Tier-3 spec-1-4; dropped before push), PR #1460 moved the 45 banners as the data-side fix.
- (constraint by claude) CAP-247 never re-attributes live history: the repo's existing `Merge {key} into main` and `Story N.M:` subjects stay classified only through the SHA/recovery allowlist and branch-scoped shapes; the templated default changes for NEW landings only. Extends 'Truth is partitioned by domain' — git stays the sole authority for merged facts, the parser just stops inventing a station for a subject that names none.
- (assumption by claude) CAP-244's in-flight window is bounded by the dispatch supervisor's own journal (`dispatch-completion` not yet written) rather than a fixed grace period — the supervisor is the externally observed witness the Constraints already require; a wall-clock grace would be a second, weaker witness.

- (event by claude) Self-validate pass 2 (preservation) on CAP-244..248: PASS. Every load-bearing claim in the Dream entry (four relaunches, the cursor wording, the flag-vs-tier-map order, the two poison shapes with their exact subjects, the banner rejection with the exact local commit) appears in exactly one CAP; the sixth human act (ledger-regression after an unattended merge) is deliberately NOT here — it is doctor's source and is seeded as `spec-pyforge-doctor:CAP-78`.

Cursor's 'out of usage'), 50.3 (`3371ab79dd`, CAP-246 — --harness outranks a dead tier-map harness; DW-FU-50-3 MRS-DISP-043 silent for uncatalogued models), 50.4 (PR #1487, `3e2859425c Merge pyforge-marshal/50-4 into main`, CAP-247 — landing evidence carries the station in every shape: `{slug}` in the repo-default merge_subject_template, parse_templated_merge_subject/classify_commit take project_slug, direct-commit shape needs branch corroboration; the per-project template overrides in marshal/doctor/herald policies retired as redundant). 50.4's review pass rewired pyforge-doctor's duplicated grammar readers; its branch then conflicted with doctor 27.5 (bare_merge.py, landed #1484 after 50.4's baseline) so dispatch land REFUSED (MRS-DISP-038) — resolved by hand (`1a5895317f`: 27.5's (sha, subject) loops + bare-form fallback kept, templated parser runs unconditionally with project_slug, bare_merge.py takes the 3-arg form); merged with the marshal-native subject, finalize replayed (ledger already done from the session's checkpoint). Catalog-only; no contract moved; scoped-stamped.
- (capability by claude) CAP-249 — verification sees the merge result. Intent: a story lands only after its verification ran against the tree `main` will actually contain — a baseline that predates a sibling's landing on overlapping files is re-verified on the merged tree (`git merge-tree --write-tree origin/main <head>`, the station's own `verify_commands`) before the PR merges, instead of being refused at land (MRS-DISP-038) or auto-merged into a runtime break; a branch whose baseline already equals origin/main verifies once, byte-identical to today. Source: docs/dreams/pyforge-marshal.md 2026-09-18 (later) item (1) — marshal 50.4 vs doctor 27.5 on `sources/marshal.py`/`sources/ledger.py`, hand-composed `1a5895317f`; the auto-merged part would have shipped `bare_merge.py`'s 2-arg call against the new 3-arg signature.
- (capability by claude) CAP-250 — the landing record follows the session's write, not the primary's directory. Intent: whatever the dispatched session wrote into its worktree's Tier-3 twin (Review Triage Log, Auto Run Result, `followup_review_recommended`, `deferred:`) is promoted into the tracked spec by finalize — the worktree's `implementation-artifacts/` is a discovery source beside the primary's, read before teardown — and a land refused after the PR was opened still journals `pr_number`/`marshal_native` (the REFUSED result no longer drops them). Source: Dream item (2) — 50.4's record promoted by hand (#1488), DW-FU-50-4 ingested by hand; `dispatch-land` projection read `pr_number: null, marshal_native: false` for a refusal after PR #1487 was opened and passed the native check.
- (capability by claude) CAP-251 — the campaign reads the ledger it just promoted. Intent: after finalize promotes the tracked ledger onto origin/main, the next campaign cycle reads that promoted state with no operator `git pull` — the primary checkout is fast-forwarded only when it is a clean `main` (`marshal refresh`'s contract; a dirty or non-main checkout is refused by name and journaled, never moved) and otherwise the ledger is read from origin/main directly; `_promote_sprint_ledger` keeps a single writer onto the remote tip. Source: Dream item (3) — every herald 23.x landing needed an operator pull before the campaign saw `done`.
- (capability by claude) CAP-252 — a blocked outcome never lands. Intent: a session that ends `blocked` (intent gap found, branch reverted to baseline, `status: blocked` written) produces no PR, no `done` promotion, and a `dispatch-blocked` journal fact carrying the spec's own reason, so the ledger tells the truth and the story is re-driven or re-minted deliberately; a revert-to-baseline plus a status flip is not git progress, and the pre-launch status guard treats `blocked` as not relaunchable without an operator decision. Source: Dream item (4) — doctor 27.3's empty branch landed as PR #1476 and promoted `27-3 → done`; the truth was written by hand and the story re-minted 27.4→27.5.
- (capability by claude) CAP-253 — MRS-DISP-043 speaks for an uncatalogued model. Intent: the fails-safe guard fires when a tier-mapped model is catalogued under no provider at all, not only when it is catalogued under a different provider — with a predicate that keeps the chosen harness's own default/alias ids (`sonnet`, `opus`, which `provider_declaring_model` documents as uncatalogued by design) silent, so a genuinely foreign model never reaches a live launch uncaught and no claude dispatch WARNs spuriously. Source: DW-FU-50-3 (50.3's own review; `cli/dispatch.py:1901-1917`, `core/model_cost.py::provider_declaring_model`).
- (capability by claude) CAP-254 — `marshal watch` follows the engine that is actually driving the station. Intent: the watch resolves a station's current run from the engine whose journal moved last — a dispatch run journaled today outranks a bmad-loop row paused in August — so a station driven by `factory dispatch` reports the dispatch run (harness, key, phase, last fact); with no dispatch run the loop row is chosen exactly as today; the choice is one pure function over both facts. Source: Dream item (6) — the fleet watch read journals directly all day on 2026-09-18 because `cli/watch.py::_gather_station` lets any live `bmad-loop list` row win.
- (capability by claude) CAP-255 — landing evidence is intent-scoped, not just station-scoped. Intent: a branch that merely mentions a story key in a station-prefixed name is not that story's landing branch; only the shapes marshal itself mints for a landing (`dispatch/<slug>/<key>`, the slug-templated merge subject, a corroborated `land/…` branch) mark a key merged, so a mint/fallout/fix branch can never poison a key — and the retrospective scan passes real branch data so `_branch_belongs_to_project` has its production caller. Source: Dream item (7) — `doctor/27-4-mint` (PR #1477) made 27.4 read landed and its first dispatch detached from a live session in one second; DW-FU-50-4 (`merged_story_keys` callers pass `branch=None`). - (decision by claude) CAP-255 refined 2026-09-19 (night) after dispatch run pyforge-marshal-20260919T202703469Z-0b70f736 returned blocked (intent gap, three independent derivations; patch preserved at implementation-artifacts/story-51-7-attempted-change-2026-09-19.patch): corroborating a station-prefixed branch by NAME is unreachable through the call path the Binding named — promotion.py::_classify_merge_subject only carries a branch when the subject already matched the GitHub PR-merge grammar, mutually exclusive with the story-direct-commit grammar — and an exact-match branch grammar would drop the only landing evidence for 83 of marshal's 347 done rows (baseline git-worktree sweep). Decision: corroborate by CONTENT, not name. A station-branch match reached through a GitHub PR-merge subject counts as a landing for the DISPATCH consumers — dispatch_supervisor's story_merged_on_main, dispatch_land's already-landed check, dispatch_land_finalize's promotion — only when the key's tracked story spec on origin/main reads status: done (a mint, fallout or fix PR merges it ready/backlog; a landing merges the promoted twin — CAP-248/250). pyforge-core's landing_evidence.py stays a pure parser: LandingEvidenceMatch exposes the branch-derived shape it matched through; marshal's core/promotion.py gains corroborated_merged_story_keys(subjects, template, slug, *, spec_status_for) and the three dispatch consumers call it. The retrospective scanners (cli/status.py, cli/deploy.py, cli/land.py, doctor sources/marshal.py) keep merged_story_keys unchanged — live history is never re-attributed, no exact-match grammar. Success: on the PR #1477 fixture 27.4 is absent from the corroborated set and present in the retrospective set; all 347 marshal done rows still classify retrospectively; a hollow landing (spec not done) is not corroborated, consistent with CAP-252. DW-FU-50-4 (branch=None in the retrospective call) is NOT closed by this CAP any more — it stays open, low, its own item. Story 51.7 spec and epics text amended to match; ledger row stays backlog for re-dispatch.

- (capability by claude) CAP-261 — the dispatch landing pays its own surface tax (CAP-239 extended from bmad-loop to marshal factory dispatch). Intent: (a) the dispatched session is told and gated the way a loop session is — the harness_bmadbuild prompt states the obligation (name every governed path you change in the owning Spec's .memlog.md and on each co-governor spec-surface names; never --write-baseline; every deferred: entry cites a repo path in location:), and the S-13.7 guard (python scripts/spec_surface_reconcile.py) is part of the session's verification, appended at render like the loop's — derived, never declared per spec — with MRS-GATE-010's binding check treating the derived guard as implicit so no tracked spec changes; (b) the landing reconciles from git facts as the safety net — before merging, dispatch_land runs the spec-surface verdict over the branch's changed governed paths and, for every Spec whose drift consists only of this branch's files, appends one event naming each path (story key, run id, merge-to-be) on that Spec's memlog and on each co-governor the detector names, scoped-stamps exactly those Specs, commits onto the dispatch branch, journals MRS-DISP-047 (the session left N paths unreconciled; the landing named them) as a warn, then merges; finalize runs deferred_work_intake --fix for the station and journals any refusal. Never a bare --write-baseline; never a stamp for a Spec whose drift includes files this branch did not change (that is someone else's drift — refuse the landing with the paths named, MRS-DISP-048); a session that reconciled itself triggers nothing and journals nothing. Success: a dispatch of a story that changes governed files lands with spec-surface green on main and no memlog edited by a human — on the 2026-09-20 fixture (26.1, 61.3, 51.11, 29.1, 61.4, 46.7's changed paths) every path is named on the right Specs; a run whose branch shares a Spec with foreign drift is refused naming the foreign paths; the harness prompt carries the obligation verbatim; the guard runs in the session's verification and MRS-GATE-010 still passes on every pre-authored spec unchanged; a deferral without location: is ingested with the location the prompt required, or journaled; pyforge-marshal-test green; the loop path (CAP-239) is byte-identical.
